# Video 28 — The reindeer mutiny

**Current status — verified 2026-09-21:** local finished export present at `../../videos_to_post/video_28.mp4`. Earlier stages and decisions below are retained as history; this check does not verify posting.

Hook plus the same six beats. v27 was approved as-is, so the garage, the approved iPhone and the no-text rule all carry over. New cast, new houses and a brand new three-beat gag so nothing repeats: Rudolph goes on strike, the herd steals the sleigh, Santa ends up riding him. The hook is a 3-2-1 curtain reveal in a Christmas-dressed warehouse.

## C01 — The 3-2-1 curtain reveal

A warehouse store dressed for Christmas, shot from inside a crowd seen strictly from behind. An enormous deep-red velvet curtain fills the centre, a man on an A-frame ladder holding each top corner.

**Status** CLIP · #1 
**Frame** `v28_c01_g1.png` 
**Clip** `v28_c01_g3.mp4` 
**Settings** gpt_image_2 · 2k · medium · 1520×2688 · 9:16 · seedance 2.5 · 720p · 5s

**References passed**

- @Image 1 · warehouse_red_curtain.png — Daniel’s warehouse red-curtain reveal photo — reproduced exactly as a written inventory (crowd from behind in the bottom third, the curtain filling the centre, a man on a ladder at each edge, red trusses and pallet racking above), with one change block: the store is now dressed for Christmas.
- @Image 2 · box_nowindow_g5.png — The approved windowless packshot, added to the CLIP only after take 2 came back with invented packaging. It governs what is printed on the boxes behind the curtain.

### Image prompt

```text
Reproduce the attached reference image EXACTLY - same vertical crop, same camera position and height, same composition, same lighting mood, same amateur phone-photo style. CHANGE ONLY ONE THING: the store is now heavily decorated for Christmas.

EVERYTHING BELOW STAYS EXACTLY AS IN THE REFERENCE:
- A vertical 9:16 photo taken on a phone from inside a crowd, held just above head height.
- FOREGROUND, bottom third of the frame: about twenty people seen strictly FROM BEHIND - backs of heads and shoulders, winter coats, hoodies, beanies and caps, packed together, slightly soft-focus, every one of them facing away from the camera and looking up. NOT ONE face is turned toward the camera.
- CENTRE of the frame: an enormous deep-red velvet curtain hung as a backdrop, very wide and very tall, filling the middle of the picture, heavy vertical folds, warm light hitting its middle so the centre glows a little brighter red than the edges. It hangs from a high horizontal rail.
- LEFT AND RIGHT EDGES: one man on each side standing high up on a tall aluminium A-frame step ladder, each holding a top corner of the curtain. They are small in the frame and seen at a distance. The man on the left wears a dark navy jacket and a cap; the man on the right wears an orange-red jacket and a cap.
- TOP of the frame: exposed industrial warehouse ceiling - corrugated metal roof deck, red-painted steel roof trusses and cross beams running overhead, hanging industrial pendant lights.
- BEHIND AND ABOVE the curtain: tall steel pallet racking stacked with cardboard boxes.

THE ONE CHANGE - dress the whole store for Christmas:
Thick green pine garland wound with red ribbon running along the red roof trusses and along the front rails of the pallet racking; warm white fairy lights strung across the ceiling beams; big red velvet bows on the racking uprights; oversized red and gold baubles hanging on ribbons from the ceiling beams; the boxes stacked on the shelves in red and green Christmas packaging; a fully decorated Christmas tree just visible at the far left behind the ladder. The decorations must sit naturally in the existing warehouse architecture - they do not change the camera, the crowd, the ladders, the men, or the size and position of the red curtain.

LOOK: a real photograph taken on a phone by someone in the crowd - natural mixed warehouse lighting, slightly blown highlights on the lit part of the curtain, mild grain, handheld imperfect framing.
DO NOT INCLUDE: any app interface, mute or speaker icon, text, captions, watermark, logo, or any face turned toward the camera. Not a render, not an illustration, no studio lighting.
```

### Video prompt

```text
THE MOST IMPORTANT INSTRUCTION, READ FIRST: the attached reference image IS the product box. Every single box revealed behind the curtain in this clip is that exact box, copied faithfully. THE BOXES ARE MATTE BLACK. They are NOT red. They are NOT green. They are NOT gold, white, patterned or generic Christmas packaging. Any red or green boxes already sitting on the shelves in the start frame are replaced by these matte black boxes in the revealed area. A wall of red boxes is a failed generation.

THE BOX, copied exactly from the reference image: a flat matte BLACK rectangular retail box. On the black front, top to bottom - a large white condensed uppercase headline LED CURTAIN LIGHTS; under it a warm gold subline Create Your Own Magic; a small red diagonal BEST SELLER ribbon flag in the top-right corner; one large photographic panel with a thin gold hairline border filling the middle and lower two thirds, showing a night photo of a suburban house facade lit with Christmas lights; and a bottom row of five small gold line icons. The box is closed and sealed - NO transparent window, NO clear plastic panel, NO cutout, nothing visible inside. The overall impression of the wall must be BLACK with white and gold print, not colourful.

One continuous take, 5 seconds, vertical 9:16, filmed on a phone held up over the heads of the crowd from exactly where the start frame is. NO zoom, NO pan, NO cuts. The crowd, the ladders and the warehouse stay in place the whole clip.

THE SCENE, unchanged from the start frame: a Christmas-decorated warehouse store. Bottom third, strictly FROM BEHIND, about twenty people in winter coats and beanies, facing away and looking up - not one face ever turns toward the camera. Centre, an enormous deep-red velvet curtain across the frame. Left edge, a man in a dark navy jacket and cap high on an aluminium A-frame ladder; right edge, a man in an orange-red jacket and cap on a matching ladder. Each grips a top corner of the curtain. Garland, fairy lights, baubles and red bows on the red roof trusses above.

ACTION - the timing must be exact and perfectly lip-synced:

0.0-0.8s - Both men shout "THREE" together. On that word they DIP the curtain DOWN and lift it straight back UP - one single down-up dip, hands dropping about forty centimetres and returning. The heavy velvet sags and bunches on the way down, stretches taut again on the way up.

0.8-1.6s - They shout "TWO" together. The same single down-up dip on the word, slightly deeper.

1.6-2.4s - They shout "ONE" together. The same single down-up dip on the word, the deepest of the three. The crowd below swells, phones going up.

2.4-3.0s - Both shout "Let's see how it comes out!" and on the last word they OPEN THEIR HANDS at the same instant and let go. The curtain collapses straight DOWN under its own weight, the top edge falling fastest, the velvet buckling and folding in on itself, the bottom pooling on the floor. Fast and heavy like wet canvas. It does NOT rise, drift sideways, fade or dissolve.

3.0-5.0s - THE REVEAL: tall steel pallet racking packed with the matte black product boxes described above, stacked face-out in tight neat rows and columns, filling the centre of the frame, their white headline and gold print catching the warehouse light against the black card. The crowd erupts - heads tipping back, arms and phones up - still every one seen only from behind. End frame: the packed racking of black boxes, fronts square to camera, print sharp and readable.

BOX SIZE: each box is a flat rectangle about 55 cm wide and 42 cm tall. FOUR stacked equal the height of a grown man. At least TEN fit side by side across the racking on every shelf and there are at least SIX shelf levels, so more than SIXTY boxes are visible at once - a dense grid of many small boxes, not a few large ones. Each is clearly much shorter than the men on the ladders. Every box is exactly the same size as every other, constant for the whole clip.

AUDIO: the two men's real voices shouting together, perfectly lip-synced and exactly on the beats above - "THREE" at 0.0, "TWO" at 0.8, "ONE" at 1.6, then "Let's see how it comes out!" - loud, excited, echoing in the metal warehouse. Under it: the fabric whump of the velvet on each dip, the ladders creaking, the crowd murmuring through the count then breaking into a real cheer and applause the instant the boxes appear. Warehouse reverb. NO music, NO whoosh or riser effects, NO narration.

REALISM: a real phone video shot in a crowd - handheld sway, a small jolt from being bumped, blown highlights on the pendant lights, crushed shadows in the roof trusses, sensor noise in the dark racking, real motion blur on the falling curtain and on raised arms, one quick autofocus hunt as the curtain drops.

BANNED: red boxes, green boxes, gold boxes, white boxes, generic or invented packaging, any box that is not the matte black box from the reference image; a transparent window or cutout in the box; warped or misspelled print; oversized or person-sized boxes; fewer than sixty boxes; empty shelves; the curtain rising on a count instead of dipping down first; the men letting go before the sentence ends; the curtain fading or dissolving instead of physically falling; any face in the crowd turning toward the camera; animation, cartoon, illustration or stylised imagery; cinematic colour grading; slow motion; any text overlay, caption, watermark or app interface.
```

### Rejected takes

- **v28_c01_g1.mp4 · take 1** — The curtain was lifted straight up instead of dipping, and the crowd’s count was out of sync with the drop, so the 3-2-1 beat landed on nothing.
- **v28_c01_g2.mp4 · take 2** — “the boxes are too big” — then, once they were resized, “everything is good but it didn’t used our boxes.” The take invented its own packaging, which is why the packshot was attached to take 3.

### What made it work

Describe the whole reference back, then isolate the one change. The prompt is an explicit “EVERYTHING BELOW STAYS EXACTLY AS IN THE REFERENCE” inventory followed by a single “THE ONE CHANGE” block. Re-describing the reference in full is what stops the camera drifting while the model adds the new elements — first take, no re-roll.

### Video prompt

```text

Same hair, same bun, same skin, same clothes, first frame to last.

BOX SPEC — TRUE IN EVERY FRAME. A matte black rigid retail box, 55 cm wide x 42 cm tall x 10 cm deep. It is BIG — much wider than the span of two outstretched hands; each of her hands covers less than a quarter of the box width, and laid on the sofa seat it spans most of one cushion. Front panel top to bottom: a large white condensed uppercase headline LED CURTAIN LIGHTS; a warm gold subline Create Your Own Magic; a red diagonal BEST SELLER ribbon flag in the top-right corner; one large photographic panel with a thin gold hairline border showing a night house facade with a glowing pixel LED Christmas tree on the garage door; a bottom row of five small gold line icons with white captions. NO transparent window, NO clear plastic panel, NO die-cut cutout, NO product visible through the front, NO glowing lights showing through the cardboard. The box is closed and sealed until the moment she opens it in scene 2.

SCENE 1 — 0.0 to 2.5s. CAMERA: HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FOCAL LENGTH, portrait, a friend filming from just behind her left shoulder at HER SHOULDER HEIGHT, tilted down only very slightly — exactly where reference image 1 was taken. NOT above her head, NOT a top-down shot. NO ZOOM IN, NO ZOOM OUT, NO PUSH IN, NO DOLLY. Handheld micro-shake only. She is at the LEFT EDGE of frame, partly cut off by it; the CARAMEL-BROWN LEATHER CHESTERFIELD SOFA is directly IN FRONT of her, running diagonally away into depth and filling the right and bottom-right of frame, its empty buttoned seat cushions facing up. The room behind her — the pale grey-washed wide-plank floor, the exposed RED BRICK accent wall, the matte black dome pendant lamp hanging above, the navy and cream cushions, the oatmeal waffle throw and the real green fir tree with red velvet ribbons and silver baubles at the right — stays exactly as it is and never changes.

THE WHOLE POINT OF SCENE 1: she carries the box round in front of the sofa, sets it flat on the seat cushion AND drops to her knees IN THE SAME CONTINUOUS MOTION. These are not two separate steps — the box going down and her body going down are one move.
0.0-0.9s — one unhurried step forward and to her right, her shoulders turning, the heavy box swinging slowly out in front of her; a loose strand comes free of the low bun and swings across her cheek, her burgundy sleeve creasing with the weight.
0.9-1.8s — she steps in front of the sofa and leans forward from the hips, lowering the box steadily toward the empty caramel leather seat cushion, arms extending, knees already bending underneath her. This lowering is SLOW and heavy — the box is big and awkward and she is being careful with it.
1.8-2.5s — the box settles flat and printed-side-up on the cushion, the old leather creaking and dimpling under it, her hands still on it, and in the same continuous motion her knees come down onto the pale grey-washed floor one after the other, her weight settling and her back rounding forward; she tucks the loose strand behind one ear and plants both palms flat on the box lid, chest rising with one excited breath.

HARD CUT AT 2.5s.

SCENE 2 — 2.5 to 5.0s. Direct continuation, same room, same second, same woman, same burgundy turtleneck, same box in exactly the same place on the same caramel leather cushion. The camera has moved about 30 degrees round to her side — a slightly more side-on three-quarter angle, a little lower, closer to the level of the sofa seat, so we now see the lid edge in profile and read the pivot clearly. Still HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FOCAL LENGTH, handheld micro-shake only, NO ZOOM.

LID MECHANISM, GEOMETRICALLY. The box is a magnetic-closure flip-top. The hinge runs along the TOP LONG EDGE of the box — the far edge away from her, at the back. The lid pivots UP and BACK on that hinge, rotating away from her and toward the back of the sofa, keeping its printed front face turned toward the camera as it rises. The inside of the lid is matte black. The lid is NEVER lifted off, NEVER removed, NEVER carried aside, NEVER set down, NEVER folded flat instantly, NEVER slid out like a drawer.
2.5-3.1s — she hooks her fingertips under the near front edge of the lid; a small snap as the magnets release and the front edge lifts a centimetre, a thin black shadow line opening slowly across the whole 55 cm width.
3.1-4.3s — she pivots the big printed lid UP and BACK on its hinge in ONE slow continuous arc with real cardboard weight, her elbows rising and her shoulders lifting with the effort, until it stands roughly vertical. This arc takes well over a full second of screen time — it travels heavily and never flips or snaps open.
4.3-5.0s — the interior is revealed exactly as reference image 3: the neatly folded BLACK LED mesh curtain lying in its tray, the coiled black lead and the small accessory items beside it, all against a matte black interior. She reaches in with both hands and touches the folded black mesh, fingers spreading it slightly, then lifts her chin, looks off toward the fir tree and breaks into a big open-mouthed grin, eyebrows up.

MOVEMENT RULE FOR BOTH SCENES: she is never frozen and never hurried. Her body is in continuous unhurried motion the whole clip — weight shifting, chest breathing, the loose strands of the bun swinging and settling, sleeves creasing, the leather cushion dimpling under the box — and her face keeps changing, eyes, eyebrows and mouth reading differently moment to moment. But every change FLOWS smoothly into the next at natural human speed. She never snaps, never jitters, never stutters between poses.

HARD RULES: exactly ONE cut, at 2.5s. The box never changes size, never changes artwork, never gains a window or a cutout. The curtain inside the box is BLACK cord with small dark bulbs — never white wire, never clear wire, never silver wire. Nobody else enters frame. ABSOLUTELY NO TEXT: no letters, no words, no numbers, no speech bubbles, no signs and no writing of any kind anywhere in the lights or anywhere in the frame. No captions, no subtitles, no watermark.

AUDIO: natural room sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Quiet warm living-room ambience, the soft thump of a heavy box on a leather cushion, knees on a wood floor, the click of magnets releasing, stiff cardboard flexing, plastic mesh rustling, one small delighted exhale.

LOOK: real iPhone UGC indoors at night. Warm mixed lamp and fairy light, slight sensor noise in the shadows, natural skin, no cinematic grade, no slow motion, no speed ramp, no fast motion. The whole clip plays at normal 1x speed from first frame to last.
```

```text
1.5s — Santa gets one boot down and skis along on his heel throwing up a wide spray of snow, his free arm flailing in circles, while the elf hurls another present overboard two-handed.
1.8s — the team splits into two pairs that criss-cross past each other and back into line, legs scissoring, and a chase of colour runs UP the whole picture from the bottom.
2.1s — Santa is flipped right over onto his back and drags along boots-first with his hat pulled down over his face, and the fallen presents on the bottom band flash colour one after another.
2.4s — the reindeer rear up on their hind legs all together for one beat, the sleigh standing almost vertical behind them, the elf hanging off the back of it by his hat, and the speed lines strobe.
2.7s — they crash back down and pour forward again, Santa slingshotted up into the air above the sleigh with all four limbs spread, and every present in the air flashes at once.
3.0s — Santa lands back down on his front and bounces twice, his remaining boot flying off, while the elf sits down in the sleigh and casually tips a whole armful of presents over the side.
3.3s — Rudolph lifts one front hoof mid-gallop and waves goodbye with it; Santa shakes a fist and the picture flashes in alternating vertical strands.
3.6s — the whole team squashes down short and wide for one beat and stretches long and thin the next, Santa snapping back and forth on the end of his arm like a whip, and the gold stars swap colour gold to white.
3.9s — Santa's grip finally slips, he tumbles backwards in a ball with his legs over his head and lands in the growing pile of presents at the bottom, the elf waving both arms overhead in triumph, and the whole picture — the team, the sleigh, Santa, the elf, every present, the speed lines and every star — flashes to maximum brightness twice in quick succession for the final beat.

COLOUR IS THE POINT: this stays a loud multicoloured light show the whole four seconds — red, green, blue, pink, orange, purple, yellow, turquoise and warm white all present and clearly separated at every moment. It must NEVER drop back to one or two colours.

Every pose change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off row by row across the grid — chunky and instantaneous, never smooth morphing, never a crossfade, never a realistic animation curve. Coloured light spills and shifts across the driveway and over the backs of the crowd with every change.

ABSOLUTELY NO TEXT: no letters, no words, no numbers, no speech bubbles, no signs and no writing of any kind anywhere in the lights or anywhere in the frame. No captions, no subtitles, no watermark.

THE CURTAIN — CRITICAL: it stays a curtain of SEPARATE HANGING VERTICAL LIGHT STRANDS, NOT a solid LED video panel, NOT a flat screen and NOT a continuous mesh. There are clear DARK GAPS between the strands and the surface behind is plainly visible through those gaps, especially in the unlit band around the picture. The bulbs are small round points of light strung along thin BLACK cords. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT.

AUDIO: natural outdoor sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Cold night air, a loose crowd chatting and laughing at different moments, scattered uneven clapping, boots in snow, breath fogging. No commentary, no voice-over.
```

### Video prompt

```text
THE FIRST FRAME OF THE VIDEO IS THE REFERENCE IMAGE, PIXEL FOR PIXEL, UNCHANGED. Do not redraw it, do not restage it, do not re-light it, do not change the lens or the angle. Start from that exact frame and animate forward from it: same night, same view shot from inside a moving car through the open passenger side window with the dark window frame at the edge of the frame, same wet reflective road with long smeared coloured reflections, same snowy verge, same street lamp at the far left, same row of neighbouring houses glowing down the street, same pale parked hatchback at the kerb, same low snow-covered wall at the right edge, same lit LED curtain on the DEEP FOREST-GREEN BOARD-AND-BATTEN GABLE with white trim showing SANTA RODEO-RIDING RUDOLPH — the pair filling the height of the lit area, seen from the side, RUDOLPH bucking hard with his head down, his antlers thrown forward, his back legs kicked straight out and up behind him and his red nose glowing; SANTA on his back thrown back at a steep angle with one hand gripping the harness and the other arm flung high in the air, legs wide, his red hat flying off above his head and his white beard blown sideways; two chunky curved motion arcs sweeping behind Rudolph's hooves, a small puff of snow bursting up under them, and small gold stars scattered around the pair.

HOW THE PICTURE IN THE LIGHTS IS DRAWN — READ THIS BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE. It is FLAT CARTOON LIGHT ART built out of individual glowing bulbs, like a chunky neon shop sign or a big pixel sprite. Thick simple outlines, flat blocks of colour, big silly cartoon faces. It is NOT a photograph. It is NOT a realistic person or animal. It is NOT 3D, NOT rendered, NOT shaded, NOT textured, NOT furry, NOT lifelike, and NOT a video projected onto the wall. If any figure could be mistaken for a photo of something real, it is wrong. Think cheap funny Christmas light display, not fine art. Fully clothed cartoon figures throughout — no bare skin, no nudity; the joke is the rodeo pose and nothing more.

CAMERA — THE CAR IS DRIVING FORWARD, TOWARD THE HOUSE. THE ONE THING THAT MUST BE RIGHT: THE HOUSE GETS BIGGER, AND IT ONLY EVER GETS BIGGER.
Size ladder, and it is strictly one-way: at 0.0s the gable wall is the size it is in the reference image. At 1.0s it is clearly bigger than at 0.0s. At 2.0s it is clearly bigger than at 1.0s. At 3.0s it is clearly bigger than at 2.0s. At 4.0s it is BIGGEST — noticeably closer than anything before it. Every frame is at least as close as the frame before it.
HARD NEGATIVE ON DIRECTION: the house NEVER gets smaller, not for a single frame. The car NEVER reverses, NEVER backs up, NEVER slows to a stop, NEVER drifts backwards away from the house. The scene is NEVER played in reverse. Nothing in the frame moves like rewound footage.
The pale parked hatchback is the proof the car is going forward: it starts small down the street, grows larger as the car approaches it, and slides gradually toward the near edge of the frame as the car draws level with it and passes it. The street lamp and the low snow-covered wall do the same. Objects the car has passed leave the frame at the near edge and never come back.
As the car advances the operator pans slowly to keep the lit gable in frame. Real vehicle motion: gentle suspension rock, slight rolling-shutter wobble, handheld drift. THIS IS DRIVING, NOT ZOOMING: no focal length change, no digital zoom, no gimbal glide, no drone move.

HARD NEGATIVE — NO PHONE IN SHOT. There is no phone, no screen, no hand, no fingers, no thumb, no arm, no sleeve, no glove, no device and no object of any kind in the foreground or along the bottom edge at any moment. Only the car's own dark window frame at the edge, exactly as in the reference image.

ABSOLUTE RULE — THE ARTWORK NEVER CHANGES SIZE AND NEVER BREAKS OUT OF THE CURTAIN. In the reference image the lit picture sits inside the curtain rectangle with a wide band of dark unlit gable wall above it, below it and at both sides. THAT EXACT SIZE AND THAT EXACT POSITION ARE LOCKED from the first frame to the last. Santa, Rudolph and the flying hat is the same number of bulbs tall and the same number of bulbs wide in the final frame as in the first frame. The artwork NEVER scales up, never grows, never inflates, never creeps outward and never drifts across the wall. There is ALWAYS a clear band of at least six unlit bulb rows of dark gable wall between the highest lit point of the artwork — the flying red hat above Santa's head — and the top edge of the curtain, and the same clear margin at the bottom and at both sides. No hoof, no antler, no boot, no hat, no motion arc and no star ever touches or crosses the edge of the curtain. Nothing lit ever appears outside the curtain rectangle — not on the roof, the white trim, the window frames, the sky, the verge, the parked car or the neighbouring houses. If a pose would raise or widen the artwork past that margin, THE POSE IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER — the artwork is never enlarged and the rectangle is never enlarged to fit it. BECAUSE IT CANNOT GROW, ALL THE ENERGY GOES SIDEWAYS, DOWNWARDS AND INTO THE BULBS: twisting, leaning, squashing, dropping, spinning, skidding, flashing and chasing — never stretching upward. The figures stay ON THE SPOT inside the rectangle; they never slide across the curtain, never march sideways and never exit one edge and re-enter at the other. All their movement is in their bodies, not in their position. SANTA'S RAISED ARM AND THE FLYING HAT ARE ALREADY AT THEIR HIGHEST POINT IN THE VERY FIRST FRAME AND NEVER GO HIGHER THAN THAT — not in any beat, not for one frame. If a buck would throw them higher, the whole pair is drawn lower down inside the rectangle instead. (The whole wall grows in frame because the car is closing in — that is the camera. The artwork does not grow inside the curtain. As the car gets closer the bulb grid just becomes coarser and the individual bulbs bigger and more countable.)

MOVEMENT — RELENTLESS, AND IT NEVER STOPS. The animation is already ON, already mid-move and already running at full speed in the VERY FIRST FRAME — there is NO still opening frame, NO pause, NO switch-on, NO ease-in — and it is STILL running hard in the last frame. SOMETHING CHANGES EVERY THREE TENTHS OF A SECOND — that is at least THIRTEEN separate changes across the four seconds — and at every single instant AT LEAST TWO things are changing at once: a whole-body pose AND a lighting effect somewhere else in the picture. There is no held pose, no still frame, no pause, no settle, no gentle drifting. Every beat is a clearly DIFFERENT WHOLE-BODY pose — arms, legs, head and body all in a new position — the figures fully redrawing between beats like an animated light-bulb sign. If two consecutive beats look similar, it is wrong.

0.0s — already mid-buck: Rudolph's head driven right down and his back legs kicked straight out behind him, Santa thrown back at a steep angle with one fist on the harness and the other arm flung high, while the two motion arcs fire bright and the snow puff bursts under the hooves.

0.6s — Rudolph drops his front knees right to the ground and Santa pitches forward onto his neck, hugging it with both arms, while every gold star twinkles out and back in a rolling wave.
0.9s — Rudolph springs back up and shakes his whole body like a wet dog, Santa rattling side to side on his back with his beard whipping across his face, and the red nose pulses twice.
1.2s — Rudolph crouches low and wide, all four legs splayed out flat, and Santa squashes down on top of him with his knees up by his ears; the whole figure strobes twice.
1.5s — Rudolph bucks the other way with his hindquarters thrown to the opposite side, Santa hanging off one hip with both legs out level, and the motion arcs flash alternately.
1.8s — Rudolph spins right round on the spot, Santa's raised arm swinging across in front of him and the hat tumbling end over end above his head, while the outline of both figures flashes brighter one bulb row at a time from the hooves upward.
2.1s — Rudolph stops dead and drops his head; Santa slides forward down his neck and ends up hanging upside down off his antlers by both knees, and a chase of colour runs UP from the hooves to the antler tips.
2.4s — Rudolph flicks his head and flips Santa back onto his seat the right way up, boots swinging wide, and every gold star swaps colour gold to white.
2.7s — Rudolph rears back onto his haunches and paws the air low in front of him, Santa leaning right back with both arms thrown out level, and the snow puff bursts to double size.
3.0s — Rudolph pogos on all four hooves at once, three fast hops on the spot, Santa bouncing up and down with his cheeks wobbling, and the harness flashes on every hop.
```

#### C1 START FRAME — v28_c01_g1.png (2026-09-14) — NEW HOOK
- Job: `c2261776-5a55-414a-b78c-0e7f91418c6e`
- `gpt_image_2`, 2k, quality medium, 9:16, count 1
- Ref (role `image`): `5f54af9f-6f4f-4213-a338-1a66e790348d` — warehouse red-curtain reveal photo
- Brief: reproduce the reference EXACTLY (crowd seen strictly from behind in the bottom third, giant
  deep-red velvet curtain filling the centre, a man on an A-frame ladder at each edge holding a top
  corner, red steel trusses and pallet racking above) and change ONE thing only: the store is now
  heavily decorated for Christmas — pine garland with red ribbon on the trusses and racking, warm
  white fairy lights across the beams, red velvet bows, oversized red/gold baubles on ribbons, red
  and green packaging on the shelves, a tree at the far left.
- Output: `clip_1/starting_frames/v28_c01_g1.png` — 1520x2688
- `clip_1/` did not exist for video 28 before this; created here.
- **Pattern that worked:** state the whole reference as an explicit "EVERYTHING BELOW STAYS EXACTLY
  AS IN THE REFERENCE" inventory, then a single "THE ONE CHANGE" block. Re-describing the reference
  in full stops the model from drifting the camera while it adds the new elements.

##### C1 VIDEO — v28_c01_g1.mp4 (2026-09-14)
- Job: `5519c54f-55cc-49b6-80c0-235ecc7d4dc0`
- `seedance_2_5`, `mode omni_reference`, 720p, 9:16, 5s, audio on,
  `declined_preset_id 24bae836-2c4a-48e0-89b6-49fcc0b21612`
- Medias: `start_image` = job `c2261776-...` (`clip_1/starting_frames/v28_c01_g1.png`)
  + box packshot `4025d4b7-70e7-4f73-a10b-4b5c396dcf45` (`product_images/box_nowindow/box_nowindow_g5.png`)
- Output: `clip_1/clips/v28_c01_g1.mp4` — 720x1280, 5.04s, audio present
- Concept: the 321 reveal from the money-prompts skill, scaled up to a warehouse. Beats —
  0.0-2.6 the two men on the ladders shout "Three... two... one... let's see how it comes out!"
  and heave the curtain up and down on each number, bounces growing · 2.6-3.1 both open their hands
  on "one", curtain collapses straight down under its own weight · 3.1-5.0 a floor-to-ceiling wall
  of our boxes is revealed, crowd erupts, all still seen from behind.
- **Box scale was written as comparisons, not centimetres alone:** "as wide as a grown man's
  shoulders", "three boxes span one ladder's spread", "a person is about four boxes tall". The bare
  55x42x10 cm figure does not survive into the render on its own.
- **Role note:** seedance_2_5 rewrites a `medias` role of `image` to `image_references`. Passing
  `image` is fine — the backend coerces it and reports the adjustment.

##### C1 VIDEO re-roll — v28_c01_g2.mp4 (2026-09-14)
- Job: `42ec09dd-50d3-454e-aee7-3ac9e2a9d9b5`. Same params, same start frame and box reference.
- Daniel rejected `_g1`: the curtain lifted up instead of dipping, the count was not in sync, and
  "the boxes are too big" (the rejected reveal had roughly four person-height boxes across the rack).
- **Fixes:**
  1. Motion direction: DOWN first, then back up — one dip per number, each deeper than the last.
  2. Sync: each number given its own 0.8s slot with the dip landing on the word, and the audio block
     repeats the same timings ("THREE" at 0.0, "TWO" at 0.8, "ONE" at 1.6).
  3. Scale: absolute centimetres do nothing. What works is a COUNT constraint — "at least TEN boxes
     side by side per shelf, at least SIX shelf levels, more than SIXTY visible, four stacked equal a
     grown man" — plus the negative "do NOT render person-sized boxes, do NOT render fewer than sixty".
- **Lesson:** to shrink an object in a render, constrain how many of them must fit in the frame rather
  than stating its dimensions.

- **Medias:** start frame `v28_c01_g1.png` (job `c2261776-5a55-414a-b78c-0e7f91418c6e`) as `start_image`,
  plus the approved packshot `product_images/box_nowindow/box_nowindow_g5.png`
  (media `4025d4b7-70e7-4f73-a10b-4b5c396dcf45`). seedance coerced role `image` -> `image_references`.

  `use_unlim: false`, `declined_preset_id: 24bae836-2c4a-48e0-89b6-49fcc0b21612`.

  curtain. The packshot reference alone did not outvote what was already visible in the frame.

  IS the product box, every revealed box is that exact box, THE BOXES ARE MATTE BLACK, not red / green /
  gold / white / generic, **and any red or green boxes already on the shelves in the start frame are
  replaced by matte black boxes in the revealed area**. "A wall of red boxes is a failed generation."
  Then the full front-panel artwork description from `box_spec.md`. BANNED now leads with red/green/gold/
  white/generic packaging and "any box that is not the matte black box from the reference image."

  on the last word of "Let's see how it comes out!" at 2.4-3.0; reveal 3.0-5.0. Box scale kept as the
  count constraint — ten across per shelf, six levels, sixty-plus visible, four stacked = one grown man.

- **Lesson:** a reference image cannot override something that is already visible in the start frame —
  you have to say explicitly that the start frame's version is replaced.

#### Redo of C4, C5 and C6 — 2026-09-14

Daniel: *"V28 - C4: the animation is too boring and simple; C5 - it's completly messed up, idk what
santa and the reind deer do on the alley, it must use our image as the starting frame; C6 - it's very
bad, there must be a lot of movement in the animation. The animation must have a lot of motion and be
very engaging"*.

| Shot | Duration | Start frame | Job id | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C4 — phone sync, garage dead-on | 4s | `v28_c04_g1.png` | `f725eb7a-a919-4140-9d0f-17dade4a9bdf` | `clip_4/clips/v28_c04_g2.mp4` |
| C5 — walking POV up the driveway | 4s | `v28_c05_g1.png` | `bb6070d8-f9d8-4567-9535-db6c2f33d8e5` | `clip_5/clips/v28_c05_g2.mp4` |
| C6 — crowd at the terracotta house | 4s | `v28_c06_g2.png` | `c392758a-ef70-4aba-ab34-42d37684a235` | `clip_6/clips/v28_c06_g2.mp4` |

Three different rejections, so three different fixes rather than one blanket "add more motion" edit.

**C5 — *"completly messed up … it must use our image as the starting frame"*.** On the first take Santa
and Rudolph had been rendered as physical 3D figures standing out on the lawn and driveway, and the house
had drifted to a dark blue building instead of the pale grey clapboard of the start frame. Three blocks
were prepended: a **reference-image-is-the-law** block naming every object that has to survive to the
last frame unchanged; an **exist-only-inside-the-lights** block stating the figures are a flat picture
made of bulbs, that the ground in front of the building stays completely empty of characters for the
whole four seconds, and that if at any single frame they read as solid bodies on the ground the shot is
wrong; and a **flat-panel** paragraph — the lit rectangle is parallel to the clapboard and moves with the
wall in the same perspective, never detaching, never becoming a stage or a doorway, nothing ever coming
out through it.

**C4 — *"too boring and simple"*.** The phone had been occupying roughly half the clip and the artwork
poses barely changed underneath it. The phone is now out by **0.9s** (scroll 0.0–0.45s, thumb to the
button 0.45–0.7s, press at 0.7s, hand gone by 0.9s), leaving more than three full seconds of pure
artwork, and the beat count went from nine to **eleven** across that shorter window, with an escalation
rule added: *"THE BIGGEST MISTAKE WOULD BE A CALM, SIMPLE, REPEATING ANIMATION … a viewer must be able to
describe FOUR OR FIVE DIFFERENT THINGS that happened in these four seconds."*

**C6 — *"very bad, there must be a lot of movement"*.** The artwork had come back as an unreadable
multicoloured blob with essentially nothing travelling. Two blocks were added. A **readability** block —
this is a wide shot so everything inside the rectangle is drawn big, chunky and separated by dark unlit
bulbs, no fine detail, no speckled noise, no glow bloom filling the gaps. And a rewritten motion preamble
built on **"FLASHING IS NOT MOVEMENT"**, a three-frame test (*"if you froze this clip at 0.0s and again
at 2.0s and again at 4.0s, the three frames must look like three completely different drawings … if a
viewer could mistake any two of those frames for the same picture, THE CLIP HAS FAILED"*) and an
amplitude floor: each travelling object crosses at least a **third** of the width or height of the lit
rectangle during the clip.

C6 bounced once with *Preset "DROWN IN MUSIC" was recommended* and went through on resubmission with
`declined_preset_id: "f1821f84-945b-4cd1-9085-1f479db0028e"`, while its two siblings went straight
through on `24bae836-…`. Same lesson as before: the id alternates per index, so read it out of the error.

---

#### The artwork in this ad

- **C4 and C5 — Rudolph on strike.** Rudolph sat flat on his backside in the snow like a stubborn dog,
  hooves folded, chin up, head turned away, red nose glowing; Santa leaning right back hauling on the
  reins with his hat knocked over his eyes and his boots skidding. C4's snap-on artwork always matches
  the C5 artwork for the same ad, so the scene picked in the app is the scene the viewer walks up to.
- **C6 — Santa's sleigh stolen.** Four reindeer bolting with Rudolph up front wearing Santa's hat and
  sunglasses, a green elf pitching presents overboard, Santa dragged along behind on his face, boots
  tumbling and presents raining down.
- **C7 — Santa rodeo-riding Rudolph.** Rudolph bucking head-down with his back legs kicked out, Santa
  thrown back at a steep angle with one fist on the harness and his hat flying off.

All three are written as **flat cartoon light art built out of individual bulbs** — an anti-realism
paragraph sits before the timeline in every prompt so the model reads it before it reads the subject.

---

#### Containment and motion

Daniel: *"the animations must not break the frame where they are placed and must contain a lot of
motion and be super engaging and interesting"*.

Every artwork-bearing prompt carries the size-lock block — the artwork is the same number of bulbs tall
and wide in the last frame as in the first, with at least six unlit bulb rows of margin on every side,
and *"if a pose would raise or widen the artwork past that margin, THE POSE IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER"*.
Because containment and "more motion" pull against each other the prompts resolve it explicitly:
*"BECAUSE IT CANNOT GROW, ALL THE ENERGY GOES SIDEWAYS, DOWNWARDS AND INTO THE BULBS."*

Motion is at least **thirteen beats at 0.3s** with two layers changing at once — a whole-body pose plus a
lighting effect elsewhere in the picture — and the rule *"if two consecutive beats look similar, it is wrong."*
C7 additionally pins Santa's raised arm and the flying hat at their highest point in the very first frame,
since a buck is the one pose that would otherwise push the artwork upward.

#### Submission notes

Each prompt is 1000–2600 words, so every shot was submitted as its own single-item batch rather than one
batch of six — a partial batch failure is not safe to retry wholesale and retrying only the failed index
is cleaner when each index is one call.

**The declined preset id alternates and must not be guessed.** Five of the six v28 indices went straight
through carrying `24bae836-2c4a-48e0-89b6-49fcc0b21612` ("IN THE DARK"). Index `280601` was bounced with
*Preset "DROWN IN MUSIC" was recommended* and needed `f1821f84-945b-4cd1-9085-1f479db0028e` instead.
A preset recommendation is **not** a refusal — resubmit the identical payload with exactly the id the
backend names.

**C4 came back `ip_detected` on the first attempt** (job `c66cf91a-93ba-4fff-b5c0-eb6ed0e27259`). Nothing
in this artwork is a protected character, and C5 carries the *identical* Rudolph-on-strike artwork and
completed first time — so this was the known transient glitch rather than a content call. Resubmitted
identical with `declined_preset_id=24bae836-2c4a-48e0-89b6-49fcc0b21612` and it went through as
`9e5f36aa-8116-4e15-aaa5-845357b02182`.

---

#### Take 3 — C4 sync + "looks like an LED panel", and the C6 motion rebuild

Daniel rejected two v28 clips: C4 for bad sync and artwork that "looks completely fake, like it's on an
LED panel, not coming from our lights", and C6 for having no motion at the start and for motion he
didn't like once it got going. Both diagnosed off ffmpeg contact sheets of the actual footage.

**C4 got the same two fixes as v27 C4**, worded for this scene (cream garage door, deep navy
board-and-batten siding, white gutter and downpipe on the left, brass barn lights, firewood at the
right). The rejected take kept the hand and phone in shot for the full four seconds and lit the wall at
~1.05s with no visible tap. The press is now a long visible thumb travel onto the play button, which
depresses with its ring flashing white; the wall is dark in the frame before contact and fully lit in
the frame of contact; and the hand exit is its own top-level HARD RULE with an explicit failure
condition. For the panel look, the full five-paragraph block from
`content/curtain_realism/anti_panel_prompt_block.md` is now pasted in verbatim — it had never actually
been pasted into a video prompt before, only summarised — plus a paragraph stating that the sky behind
the characters is not drawn at all.

**C6, the dead opening second** had the same impossible-instruction root cause as v27 C5 and C7 and got
the same fix: the checkable 0.2s / 0.4s / 0.6s rule, the opening pose abandoned within two frames, no
ease-in or warm-up, and a front-loaded first second of seven complete pose changes.

**C6, "I don't like the motion".** On the rejected take the artwork collapsed into a thin band of
multicoloured confetti — everything blinking, nothing travelling. That was fixed in the motion design,
not in the artwork: presents in the air are capped at two at any instant (explicitly *not* a shower,
not a rain of small objects, not confetti), the continuous star chase and twinkle is banned, simultaneous
lighting effects are capped at two, a new rule states that the motion is **bodies travelling, not lights
blinking**, and three large-amplitude travel beats were written in — at 0.7s the team lurches left and
drags Santa from the left edge to the middle, at 2.5s it pours down to the bottom right and swings him
across, at 3.4s it hauls to the top right and flings him the full width back to the left.

**The C6 start frame was deliberately left alone.** `v28_c06_g2.png` was inspected at full size and its
artwork is readable and correct — the confetti failure came out of the video generation, not the frame —
so regenerating it would have changed something Daniel had not asked to change.

| C6 — crowd, Santa dragged behind the sleigh | 4s | `v28_c06_g2.png` | `b13aeb53-ae19-482f-8de8-164de1a3bde4` | `clip_6/clips/v28_c06_g3.mp4` |

C6 bounced once on the preset recommendation (`DROWN IN MUSIC`) and went through on
`f1821f84-945b-4cd1-9085-1f479db0028e`; C4 went through first try on
`24bae836-2c4a-48e0-89b6-49fcc0b21612`. Board copies mirrored to `all_clips/v28_cNN.mp4`.

#### Upscale — 2026-09-14 (ByteDance Upscale, 2K / 30fps / common)

`upscale_video` · `provider: "bytedance"` · `resolution: "2k"` · `fps: 30` · `preset: "common"`.
Source `width`/`height` passed per clip (C1 is 720×1280, C2–C7 are 1080×1920). Output is **1440×2560 @ 30fps**
for every shot, audio preserved. Workspace **Oneofone1**, 1.6 credits each.

The connector has **no Standard/Pro model-version parameter** — the screenshot's *Pro* toggle is UI-only, so these
ran at whatever tier the API defaults to. Everything else matches the screenshot exactly.

Sources were re-uploaded from disk (`media_upload` → `media_confirm`) rather than referenced by their generation
job id, because the tables above are stale for the newest takes and C1 was never logged. Upscaling the on-disk file
guarantees it ran on the exact take in `clips/`.
