## Prompt-file versions not on the board

These are the prompts as they sit in `video_prompts/`. Where a shot above shows a different text, this is a different pass at the same shot - usually a later rewrite - so both are kept.

### `v28_c04.txt`

```text
Animate the reference image exactly as it is. The first frame of the video IS the reference image, unchanged — same night, same DETACHED TWO-CAR GARAGE seen dead-on and flat, same wide WARM CREAM sectional double garage door with its panel seams and its row of small square windows across the top, same DEEP NAVY-BLUE board-and-batten siding, same WHITE gutter and WHITE downpipe on the LEFT, same BRASS BARN LIGHT glowing either side of the door, same stack of split firewood under its shed roof at the RIGHT, same tall spruce beyond the right corner, same gravel-and-snow driveway in the foreground, same UNLIT LED curtain hanging in thin black vertical strands down across the door, same hand coming up from the bottom holding a phone up big in the lower part of frame, same app screen showing a dark navy grid of small colourful scene tiles with a BIG ROUND BLUE PLAY BUTTON near the bottom of the screen.

CAMERA — HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FRAMING. Portrait, handheld micro-shake only. The camera stays DEAD-ON SQUARE to the garage door the whole four seconds — it never swings round to an oblique angle, never turns to a corner view, never tilts up. ABSOLUTELY NO ZOOM IN, NO ZOOM OUT, NO PUSH IN, NO PULL BACK, NO DOLLY. The garage and the phone stay exactly the same size in frame throughout.

THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT THING IN THIS CLIP IS THE SYNC — AND THE SYNC ONLY READS IF THE PRESS IS VISIBLE. A viewer must be able to SEE the thumb arrive on the button and SEE the button react, otherwise the lights just come on by themselves and the whole shot is worthless. So the press is staged BIG AND OBVIOUS: between 0.45s and 0.7s the thumb travels a LONG, CLEARLY VISIBLE distance down the screen and lands squarely on the BIG ROUND BLUE PLAY BUTTON; at 0.7s the button visibly DEPRESSES, its blue ring FLASHES WHITE, and the app screen changes state behind it in the same instant. ON THAT SAME SINGLE FRAME the garage door lights up. Not a frame early, not a frame late, no gap, no delay, no anticipation, no lag, no crossfade. One frame dark, the next frame fully lit at maximum brightness. The press and the light are ONE event.

HARD RULE — THE ARM DROPS THE INSTANT HE PRESSES AND IS GONE WITHIN TWO TENTHS OF A SECOND. The press and the drop are one single continuous movement: the thumb hits the button and the whole arm immediately falls straight down and out of the bottom of the frame like a dropped weight, fast and unbroken, still holding the phone. It does NOT linger. It does NOT slow on the way out. It does NOT hold the phone up to admire the lights. It does NOT tilt the phone toward the wall. It does NOT stay in shot for even a quarter of a second after the press. BY 0.9 SECONDS THERE IS NO HAND, NO PHONE, NO SCREEN, NO THUMB, NO FINGER, NO WRIST, NO ARM AND NO SLEEVE ANYWHERE IN THE FRAME, and no part of them ever reappears — from 0.9s to the last frame the bottom of the frame is empty snowy driveway and nothing else. If the phone is still visible at 1.0s, THE CLIP HAS FAILED.

THE PHONE PART IS VERY SHORT. THE LIT PART IS ALMOST THE WHOLE CLIP. From 0.9s to the very last frame — MORE THAN THREE FULL SECONDS — there is nothing whatsoever in the foreground and the lights are on and animating flat out.

ACTION TIMELINE (4 seconds, one continuous take, no cuts):
0.0-0.45s — THE DOOR IS DARK. A SHORT SCROLL, NOT A LONG ONE. The thumb comes in from the bottom and gives the grid of scene tiles ONE small quick flick: the tiles slide up by roughly ONE ROW only, a single fresh row of new scene thumbnails comes into view from the bottom, the list overshoots slightly and settles back with a small bounce. That is the entire scroll. NO long scroll, NO repeated flicks, NO second flick, NO rows racing past. One tile in the grid keeps its bright highlight ring the whole time — the scene is already chosen.
0.45-0.7s — THE DOOR IS STILL DARK. The thumb travels a long, clearly visible distance straight down the screen to the BIG ROUND BLUE PLAY BUTTON at the bottom of the app and lands squarely on it. This travel is big enough to read plainly in a single glance.
0.7s — THE THUMB PRESSES PLAY. On that exact frame the button visibly DEPRESSES, its blue ring FLASHES WHITE, the app screen changes state behind it, AND the entire curtain SNAPS ON at full brightness in the very same single frame. NO fade, NO dissolve, NO ramp, NO brightness curve, NO bulbs lighting one by one, NO sweep across the door, NO pause between the press and the light.
THE ARTWORK THAT APPEARS: a big jolly white-bearded SANTA in his red fur-trimmed suit and red hat, seen from the front, wearing dark sunglasses, lounging back in a steaming round wooden HOT TUB that fills the lower half of the lit area. One arm is raised, the other hand holds a tall cocktail with a tiny umbrella in it. Fat round bubbles rise and pop around him, curls of white steam lift off the water, and a few small gold stars and white snowflakes are scattered in the dark around the tub. Colours are bright red, white, warm gold, pale blue water and a little green on the umbrella, against the dark unlit door. There is no chimney, no goblin, no sack and no presents in this clip.
0.7-0.9s — the arm falls straight down and out of the bottom of frame in one fast unbroken drop and NEVER returns.
0.9-4.0s — the lit garage door alone, and the animation runs flat out to the last frame.

THE SKY BEHIND HIM IS NOT DRAWN. There is NO blue background, NO coloured backdrop, NO filled sky, NO solid panel of colour behind the figure. Behind and around Santa and the tub the curtain is simply UNLIT — dark cream garage door showing through, with the unlit bulbs reading as tiny dull grey dots and the black cords as faint vertical lines. Only Santa, the tub, the water, the bubbles, the steam, the stars and the snowflakes are lit. A saturated rectangle of background colour is the single clearest sign this is a video screen rather than a light curtain, and it must not appear anywhere.

MOVEMENT — RELENTLESS, AND IT NEVER STOPS. From the moment the door snaps on, SOMETHING CHANGES EVERY THREE TENTHS OF A SECOND — that is at least TEN separate changes between 0.9s and the last frame — and at every single instant AT LEAST TWO things are changing at once: Santa's whole-body pose AND a lighting effect somewhere else in the picture. There is no held pose, no still frame, no pause, no settle, no gentle drifting. Every pose is a clearly different WHOLE-BODY pose, the figure fully redrawing between beats like an animated light-bulb sign.
0.9s — he lands out of the snap-on already mid-move, the cocktail swung up in front of his chest and tipped toward the viewer, cheeks ballooned out fat mid-slurp, and the round bubbles fire on one after another around the rim of the tub.
1.2s — he throws both arms out level to the sides with his elbows bent, head tipped back, mouth a big open O, while the water in the tub chases colour left to right.
1.5s — he twists hard to one side, one shoulder driven forward, the cocktail held out level, and his sunglasses flash bright white twice.
1.8s — he twists hard the other way, the other shoulder forward, his beard swinging across, and a bright chase of colour runs down his whole body from hat to hands.
2.1s — he drops down low into the water until only his eyes, his hat and the raised cocktail are left showing, and every bubble twinkles out and back in a rolling wave from left to right.
2.4s — he pops straight back up with both arms braced on the rim of the tub and shimmies his shoulders left-right-left, the curls of steam flashing alternately.
2.7s — he kicks one boot up out of the water low and short to the side, arms bent and swinging opposite, his eyes wide open circles, and the wooden staves of the tub swap colour warm brown to red.
3.0s — he leans right back with both hands behind his head, elbows wide, one eye closed in a wink, and a crown of round droplets fires on all around the tub at once.
3.3s — he claps both hands together in front of his chest twice fast, his head bobbing with each clap, and the white fur trim on his suit and hat flashes brighter.
3.6s — he bounces in the tub twice, both arms bent up beside his head, the whole figure squashing and stretching with each bounce, and the stars and snowflakes flash on and off in alternating pairs.
3.9s — he lands square, cocktail raised, and the whole picture — figure, tub, water, bubbles, steam, stars and snowflakes — flashes to maximum brightness twice in quick succession for the final beat.
Every pose change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off row by row across the grid — chunky and instantaneous, never smooth morphing, never a crossfade. Coloured light spills and shifts across the snow below with every change.

ABSOLUTE RULE — THE ARTWORK NEVER CHANGES SIZE. When the door snaps on, the lit picture sits inside the curtain rectangle with a wide band of dark unlit cream door above it, below it and at both sides. THAT EXACT SIZE AND THAT EXACT POSITION ARE LOCKED from 0.7s to the last frame. Santa and the tub are the same number of bulbs tall and the same number of bulbs wide in the final frame as in the frame they first appear. He NEVER scales up, never grows, never inflates, never creeps upward, never fills more of the curtain than he does at the start. There is ALWAYS a clear band of at least six unlit bulb rows of dark door between the highest lit point of the artwork and the top edge of the curtain, and the same clear margin at the bottom and at both sides. No hand, no hat, no cocktail, no bubble, no droplet, no steam wisp and no star ever touches or crosses the edge of the curtain, and nothing lit ever appears on the navy siding, the white gutter, the roof, the sky, the firewood stack, the spruce or the driveway. If a pose would raise him or widen him past that margin, THE POSE IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER — the artwork is never enlarged and the rectangle is never enlarged to fit it. BECAUSE HE CANNOT GROW, ALL HIS ENERGY GOES SIDEWAYS, DOWNWARDS AND INTO THE BULBS: twisting, sinking, leaning, bouncing, kicking low, flashing and chasing — never stretching upward. He also stays ON THE SPOT and centred; he never walks or slides sideways across the curtain.

CRITICAL — HOW THE LIGHTS MUST BE BUILT. The picture on the garage door is NOT an LED video wall, NOT a screen, NOT a projection. It is a hanging curtain of LED string lights: many thin vertical BLACK wires hung side by side from a header cable pinned under the garage lintel, each wire carrying small round bulbs. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT. The horizontal gap between neighbouring strands is visibly WIDER than the vertical gap between bulbs on the same strand, so the grid is tall and rectangular — never a square pixel matrix.
The curtain is SEE-THROUGH. Through the gaps between strands you clearly read the garage door behind it: its panel seams, its trim, the row of small square windows, the faint shadows of the wires falling on it. The unlit areas are NOT a solid coloured field — they are simply dark door, with the unlit bulbs showing as tiny dull grey dots and the thin BLACK wires as faint vertical lines.
Every lit bulb is a tiny point source: blown-out white core, coloured halo bleeding around it, a small soft star flare, individually countable. The artwork is extremely low resolution and blocky — chunky stepped edges, no smooth curves, no thin outlines, no small facial detail, no gradients, no anti-aliasing.
The curtain hangs a few inches proud of the door: the strands sag slightly, each strand ends at a slightly different height so the bottom hem is uneven and wavy, a faint breeze tilts the sheet, and the glow bounces back off the door behind it as a soft halo. Light spills onto the snow in a speckled uneven pattern, not a flat even wash.
ABSOLUTELY NO: LED video wall, TV screen, monitor, jumbotron, projector image, black backing panel, bezel or frame, dense square pixel grid, solid saturated background field, smooth clean vector artwork. Real amateur photo, natural noise, no CGI polish.

HOW THE PICTURE IN THE LIGHTS IS DRAWN: FLAT CARTOON LIGHT ART built out of individual glowing bulbs, like a chunky neon shop sign or a big pixel sprite. Thick simple outlines, flat blocks of colour, a big silly cartoon face. It is NOT a photograph, NOT a realistic person, NOT 3D, NOT rendered, NOT shaded, NOT textured, NOT lifelike, and NOT a video projected onto the door. Think cheap funny Christmas light display, not fine art.

HARD RULES: NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO SIGNS anywhere in the light artwork, and no captions, subtitles or watermark anywhere in the video. Nobody walks into frame.

AUDIO: natural outdoor sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Cold night air, a soft tap as the thumb hits the screen, the faint electrical hum and tick of a big light display, one quiet breath. No voice, nobody speaks.

LOOK: real iPhone night video, handheld, tack-sharp, candid UGC. Slight sensor noise in the shadows, no cinematic grade, no slow motion, no speed ramp. No text overlay, no watermark, no captions, no logo.
```

### `v28_c05.txt`

```text
THE REFERENCE IMAGE IS THE LAW — READ THIS BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE. Frame one of this video IS the reference image, PIXEL FOR PIXEL. Do not redraw the scene, do not restage it, do not re-light it, do not invent a different building, do not change the colour of the building, do not change the lens, the angle, the framing or the time of night. The building in this video is the building in the reference image and no other building. Same night, same PALE GREY CLAPBOARD TWO-STOREY house with black trim, black window shutters and a black front door, same small white porch at the right with its little lit tree, same row of snow-capped shrubs with warm lights along the left, same wide cleared driveway with tyre tracks in the snow, same neighbouring houses and dark spruce beyond. All of it is still there in the last frame, in the same place, the same colour and the same size as in the first frame.

THE LIT ARTWORK — RUDOLPH ON STRIKE. Across the front wall the big lit LED curtain shows, on the RIGHT of the lit area, RUDOLPH sat flat on his backside in the snow like a stubborn dog, front hooves folded across his chest, chin tipped up and his head turned pointedly away, his big round RED NOSE glowing bright, a harness still round his shoulders; and on the LEFT, SANTA leaning right back at a steep angle hauling on the reins with both hands, his black boots skidding forward and throwing up two little sprays of snow, his red hat knocked down over his eyes; the taut reins stretched straight between them across the middle of the picture, with white snowflakes and small gold stars scattered around the pair.

SANTA AND RUDOLPH EXIST ONLY INSIDE THE LIGHTS. They are NOT characters in this scene. They are NOT real, NOT solid, NOT three-dimensional and NOT present in the world of the shot. They are A FLAT PICTURE MADE OF LIGHT BULBS hanging against the wall, confined to the lit rectangle and to nowhere else. THERE IS NEVER A SANTA STANDING ON THE GROUND. THERE IS NEVER A REINDEER ON THE DRIVEWAY, THE PATH, THE LAWN, THE PORCH, THE ROOF OR ANYWHERE IN FRONT OF THE HOUSE. No figure ever steps out of the wall, walks onto the ground, stands in the snow, casts a shadow on the snow or blocks any part of the building. The ground and the air in front of the building stay COMPLETELY EMPTY of characters for the whole four seconds.

HOW THE PICTURE IN THE LIGHTS IS DRAWN — It is FLAT CARTOON LIGHT ART built out of individual glowing bulbs, like a chunky neon shop sign or a big pixel sprite. Thick simple outlines, flat blocks of colour, big silly cartoon faces. It is NOT a photograph. It is NOT a realistic person or animal. It is NOT 3D, NOT rendered, NOT shaded, NOT textured, NOT furry, NOT lifelike, and NOT a video projected onto the wall. Think cheap funny Christmas light display, not fine art.

CAMERA — FIRST-PERSON POV. The person filming is WALKING FORWARD up the cleared driveway toward the house, one continuous four-second take. THE CAMERA ITSELF IS THE PHONE, so we see ONLY what the walker sees. The house grows steadily and continuously larger and NEVER recedes. Natural bodyweight rise-and-fall with each stride, a slight left-right sway, strong parallax — the row of snow-capped shrubs, the tyre tracks and the snow banks at the edges of frame sweep past and out of frame much faster than the house moves. THIS IS WALKING, NOT ZOOMING: no focal length change, no digital zoom, no push-in, no dolly rig smoothness, no gimbal glide, no drone move. Handheld, breathing, slightly imperfect.

HARD NEGATIVE — NO PHONE IN SHOT. There is no phone, no screen, no hand, no fingers, no thumb, no arm, no sleeve, no glove, no device and no object of any kind in the foreground or along the bottom edge at any moment.

CONTINUOUS MOTION — THIS IS THE ONE THING THAT WENT WRONG LAST TIME AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT RULE IN THIS PROMPT.
The last version cut between unrelated drawings — Santa in one pose, then suddenly a completely different unconnected pose, then another. That reads as a broken slideshow and it is exactly what must not happen.
The figures move the way a hand-drawn cartoon moves: EVERY FRAME IS AN IN-BETWEEN OF THE FRAME BEFORE IT. Arms, legs, heads and bodies TRAVEL THROUGH each movement along a smooth arc. A limb is NEVER teleported from one position to another. The picture NEVER cuts, NEVER snaps to an unrelated drawing, NEVER jumps, NEVER resets to the opening pose, NEVER freezes, NEVER pauses, NEVER settles and NEVER restarts as a loop.
It is ONE unbroken piece of comic business — a tug of war that escalates — running from the very first frame to the very last, and it is still running in the last frame. The story connects: each thing Santa does is caused by the thing that just happened to him.
THE TEST: play any half second of this clip and the motion inside it must FLOW — a body carries through, an arm sweeps, the reins snap and whip along their length. If you can see the picture blink from one drawing to a different unrelated drawing, THE CLIP HAS FAILED.
AND IT IS BIG — BUT BIG IN PLACE, NOT BIG ACROSS THE WALL. At every moment at least one figure is moving its whole body hard: twisting, dropping, toppling, leaning, squashing, hauling, shoving. That energy goes SIDEWAYS, DOWNWARDS AND INTO THE BULBS, never outward past the edges of the picture and never upward. Neither figure ever travels more than a fraction of the picture's width, and neither ever leaves the SAFE BOX described below. FLASHING IS NOT MOVEMENT: colour chases and strobes are allowed but they are secondary, and no more than one is running at a time. What the viewer sees is bodies moving, not lights blinking.
IT IS ALREADY RUNNING IN FRAME ONE: mid-haul, mid-skid, at full speed. No still opening frame, no switch-on, no ease-in, no warm-up.

THE TUG OF WAR, PHASE BY PHASE — ONE CONTINUOUS STORY:
0.0-0.6s — already mid-haul: Santa leans back further and further, almost horizontal, both boots skidding steadily forward across the picture and throwing up growing sprays of snow, the reins stretching tighter and tighter. Rudolph doesn't budge — he only tips his chin higher and higher as Santa strains, his red nose swelling brighter.
0.6-1.2s — Rudolph suddenly shunts backwards, and Santa is hauled forward in one continuous lurch of about one body-width, up onto his toes, arms stretching out long in front of him, the slack reins whipping through a big travelling wave. He stays well inside the left half of the picture throughout — he does not cross the middle and he never nears the edge.
1.2-1.8s — Santa's boots lose their grip and he goes over backwards in one smooth topple — feet rising, body rotating, landing hard on his backside with both boots swinging up in the air — then rolls straight on over onto all fours without stopping, his hat sliding all the way down over his eyes on the way.
1.8-2.4s — still on his knees he crawls round behind Rudolph in one continuous move and starts shoving his backside with both hands, pushing with his whole body, his own boots slipping backwards under him a little further with every shove, while Rudolph slides sideways just a hand's width, plants himself and slowly blows an enormous raspberry, his cheeks ballooning out.
2.4-3.0s — Santa gives up, straightens up, pulls a big orange carrot out from behind his back and dangles it, swinging it slowly back and forth. Rudolph's head follows the carrot right to left, right to left, then he turns his nose away in one slow deliberate sweep and closes both eyes.
3.0-3.5s — Santa throws the carrot down, plants both fists on his hips, stamps one boot twice, grabs the reins again and hauls with one foot braced on Rudolph's backside, leaning right out sideways further and further.
3.5-4.0s — Rudolph stands straight up all at once, the reins go slack and Santa pitches forward onto his front and skids a short way along the bottom of the picture with his boots kicked up behind him — a short skid of about one body-length, ending well clear of the right-hand edge, never sliding off the picture. Rudolph sits calmly back down again. Santa picks himself up and drops down beside him in exactly the same sulking pose — arms folded, chin up — and Rudolph is still turning his head to wink one big cartoon eye as the clip ends. Everything is still moving in the very last frame.

Every change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off across the grid — chunky and stepped, like an animated light-bulb sign — but the movement it describes is continuous and flowing, never a jump between unrelated drawings. Coloured light spills and shifts across the snow and the cleared driveway as the picture moves.

ABSOLUTE RULE — THE ARTWORK NEVER CHANGES SIZE AND NEVER BREAKS OUT OF THE CURTAIN. In the reference image the lit picture sits inside the curtain rectangle with a wide band of dark unlit clapboard wall above it, below it and at both sides. THAT EXACT SIZE AND THAT EXACT POSITION ARE LOCKED from the first frame to the last. Santa, Rudolph and the reins are the same number of bulbs tall and the same number of bulbs wide in the final frame as in the first frame. The artwork NEVER scales up, never grows, never inflates, never creeps outward and never drifts across the wall. There is ALWAYS a clear band of at least six unlit bulb rows of dark clapboard wall between the highest lit point of the artwork — the bobble of Santa's hat — and the top edge of the curtain, and the same clear margin at the bottom and at both sides. No boot, no hoof, no antler, no rein, no carrot, no bobble, no snowflake and no star ever touches or crosses the edge of the curtain. Nothing lit ever appears outside the curtain rectangle — not on the black trim, the shutters, the window frames, the porch, the roof, the sky, the shrubs or the neighbouring houses. If a pose would raise or widen the artwork past that margin, THE POSE IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER. The figures travel only inside the rectangle; they never exit one edge and re-enter at the other. As the camera walks closer the bulb grid simply becomes coarser and the individual bulbs become bigger and more countable — the artwork does not gain detail and does not gain size.

THE SAFE BOX — THIS IS THE RULE THAT WENT WRONG LAST TIME. LAST TIME THE ANIMATION SPILLED OUT PAST THE EDGES OF THE LIGHTS AND ONTO THE WALL. IT MUST NOT HAPPEN AGAIN.
Imagine an inner rectangle drawn inside the lit curtain, leaving a wide dark border of at least six unlit bulb rows on all four sides. That inner rectangle is THE SAFE BOX. EVERY lit thing in this clip — Santa, Rudolph, the reins, the harness, the carrot, the hat, the boots, the hooves, the antlers, the snow sprays, the snowflakes and the stars — lives entirely inside the SAFE BOX for all four seconds, in every single frame, in every single pose. Nothing ever touches the edge of the SAFE BOX, let alone crosses it. No limb pokes out. No rein stretches out. No spray of snow flicks out. No star drifts out.
The SAFE BOX does not move and does not stretch. It is fixed to the wall and it scales with the wall as the camera walks in — exactly as the curtain does, exactly as the clapboard does. It never widens to accommodate a pose, it never slides across the wall, and it never lets an action run off one side.
BECAUSE THE FIGURES CANNOT LEAVE THE BOX, ALL THE ENERGY GOES SIDEWAYS, DOWNWARDS AND INTO THE BULBS — toppling, crouching, leaning, twisting, shoving, squashing, flashing and chasing — never reaching outward and never stretching upward. Santa's hands never go above the top of his own hat. Rudolph's antlers never rise. A pose that would break the box IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER instead. If ANY lit element crosses the edge of the curtain onto the clapboard, the black trim, the shutters, the porch, the roof or the sky at any point, THE CLIP HAS FAILED.

THE LIT RECTANGLE IS A FLAT PANEL OF LIGHT ON A FLAT WALL. It is parallel to the clapboard behind it and it moves with the wall exactly as the wall moves as the camera walks in — same perspective, same skew, same foreshortening. It never detaches from the wall, never floats forward, never becomes a stage, never becomes a doorway, never becomes a hole, and nothing ever comes out through it.

THE CURTAIN — CRITICAL: it stays a curtain of SEPARATE HANGING VERTICAL LIGHT STRANDS, NOT a solid LED video panel, NOT a flat screen and NOT a continuous mesh. There are clear DARK GAPS between the strands and the clapboard behind is plainly visible through those gaps, especially in the unlit band around the picture. The horizontal gap between neighbouring strands is visibly wider than the vertical gap between bulbs on the same strand, so the grid is tall and rectangular, never a square pixel matrix. The bulbs are small round points of light strung along thin BLACK cords. The unlit area is NOT a solid coloured field — it is simply dark wall with dull grey unlit bulbs and faint dark cords, and there is NO filled background, NO coloured backdrop and NO saturated panel of colour behind the figures at any time. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT.

ABSOLUTELY NO TEXT: no letters, no words, no numbers, no speech bubbles, no signs and no writing of any kind anywhere in the lights or anywhere in the frame. No captions, no subtitles, no watermark.

AUDIO: natural outdoor sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Cold night air, boots compressing snow at a walking rhythm, the faint electrical hum and tick of a big light display, one quiet breath. No voice.

LOOK: real iPhone night video, handheld, tack-sharp, candid UGC. Slight sensor noise in the shadows, no cinematic grade, no slow motion, no speed ramp. No text overlay, no watermark, no captions, no logo.
```

### `v28_c06.txt`

```text
Wholesome neighbourhood Christmas scene, warm and jolly. Animate the reference image exactly as it is. The first frame of the video IS the reference image, unchanged — same WARM TERRACOTTA STUCCO house with the round attic vent in the gable, same WHITE DOUBLE GARAGE DOOR, same wall sconce glowing at the right, same dark hedge at the left edge, same snow-covered driveway throwing coloured light back, same crowd of neighbours seen FROM BEHIND standing on the snow in winter coats and bobble hats along the bottom of frame, same lit LED curtain across the garage door showing SANTA BEING DRAGGED BEHIND HIS OWN STOLEN SLEIGH — four reindeer in a galloping line across the upper right tearing away to the upper right with the big red-and-gold sleigh behind them, legs kicked out flat, RUDOLPH at the front with a glowing red nose, SANTA'S RED HAT on his own head and tiny black sunglasses on his face; SANTA himself NOT in the sleigh but hanging off the back of it by ONE HAND at the LEFT, body stretched out horizontal in the air, legs trailing, bald head shining white, beard blown sideways, mouth wide open, both black boots flown off and tumbling separately through the air; eight or nine presents in emerald green, hot pink, electric blue, orange, purple, yellow, turquoise and lime raining down across the middle and left on curved paths, two of them burst open; a cheeky GREEN ELF standing up in the back of the sleigh with both arms in the air throwing another present overboard; curved multicoloured speed lines streaking behind the sleigh and bright stars in many colours scattered right across the lit area.

THE REFERENCE IMAGE IS THE LAW — READ THIS BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE. Frame one of this video IS the reference image, PIXEL FOR PIXEL. Do not redraw the scene, do not restage it, do not re-light it, do not invent a different building, do not change the colour of the building, do not change the lens, the angle, the framing or the time of night. The building in this video is the building in the reference image and no other building. Same WARM TERRACOTTA STUCCO house with the round attic vent in the gable, same WHITE DOUBLE GARAGE DOOR, same wall sconce glowing at the right, same dark hedge at the left edge, same snow-covered driveway, same crowd of neighbours seen FROM BEHIND along the bottom of frame. All of it is still there in the last frame, in the same place, the same colour and the same size as in the first frame.

SANTA, THE REINDEER AND THE ELF EXIST ONLY INSIDE THE LIGHTS. They are NOT characters in this scene. They are NOT real, NOT solid, NOT three-dimensional and NOT present in the world of the shot. They are A FLAT PICTURE MADE OF LIGHT BULBS hanging against the wall, confined to the lit rectangle shown in the reference image and to nowhere else. THERE IS NEVER A SANTA STANDING ON THE GROUND. THERE IS NEVER A REINDEER ON THE DRIVEWAY, THE SNOW, THE ROOF, AMONG THE CROWD OR ANYWHERE IN FRONT OF THE HOUSE. No figure ever steps out of the wall, walks onto the ground, stands in the snow, casts a shadow on the snow or blocks any part of the building. No present ever leaves the wall, falls onto the driveway or lands in the snow. The ground and the air in front of the building stay COMPLETELY EMPTY of characters and objects for the whole four seconds — the only things on the ground are the things already on the ground in the reference image.

THE ARTWORK MUST STAY READABLE AT A DISTANCE — DO NOT LET IT TURN INTO A COLOURED BLUR. This is a wide shot, so the lit rectangle is small in frame. Every element inside it is therefore drawn BIG, CHUNKY and WELL SEPARATED: thick heavy outlines several bulbs wide, large flat blocks of a SINGLE colour inside each shape, and a clear band of DARK UNLIT BULBS between every figure and the next so the silhouettes never touch or merge. Each reindeer is one solid readable animal shape. The sleigh is one solid readable shape. Santa is one solid readable shape. The elf is one solid readable shape. EACH PRESENT IS ONE SOLID CHUNKY BOX OF A SINGLE FLAT COLOUR with a contrasting ribbon cross on it — big enough to read clearly from the bottom of the driveway. NO fine detail, NO thin lines, NO speckled multicoloured noise, NO confetti of tiny dots, NO gradients, NO glow bloom filling the gaps between shapes. If the lit rectangle ever reads as a mottled multicoloured blob instead of a clear cartoon picture a stranger could describe out loud, IT IS WRONG. Fewer, bigger, cleaner shapes beat more detail every single time.

HOW THE PICTURE IN THE LIGHTS IS DRAWN. It is FLAT CARTOON LIGHT ART built out of individual glowing bulbs, like a chunky neon shop sign or a big pixel sprite. Thick simple outlines, flat blocks of colour, big silly cartoon faces. It is NOT a photograph. It is NOT a realistic person or animal. It is NOT 3D, NOT rendered, NOT shaded, NOT textured, NOT furry, NOT lifelike, and NOT a video projected onto the wall. Think cheap funny Christmas light display, not fine art.

CAMERA — HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FRAMING. Portrait, filmed from behind the group at the bottom of the driveway. Handheld micro-shake only — a few pixels of organic drift. ABSOLUTELY NO ZOOM IN, NO ZOOM OUT, NO PUSH IN, NO PULL BACK, NO DOLLY, NO CRANE. The house and the garage door stay exactly the same size in frame from the first frame to the last.

CONTINUOUS MOTION — THIS IS THE ONE THING THAT WENT WRONG LAST TIME AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT RULE IN THIS PROMPT.
The last version cut between unrelated drawings — the team in one arrangement, then suddenly a completely different unconnected arrangement, then another — and the presents just sat there while colours flickered over them. That reads as a broken slideshow and it is exactly what must not happen.
Everything in the lit picture moves the way a hand-drawn cartoon moves: EVERY FRAME IS AN IN-BETWEEN OF THE FRAME BEFORE IT. Legs, bodies, the sleigh, Santa, the elf, the boots and above all THE PRESENTS TRAVEL THROUGH each movement along a smooth arc. Nothing is ever teleported from one position to another. The picture NEVER cuts, NEVER snaps to an unrelated drawing, NEVER jumps, NEVER resets to the opening arrangement, NEVER freezes, NEVER pauses, NEVER settles and NEVER restarts as a loop.
It is ONE unbroken piece of comic business — a runaway sleigh dragging Santa, escalating — running from the very first frame to the very last, and it is still running in the last frame. Each thing that happens is caused by the thing that just happened.
THE TEST: play any half second of this clip and the motion inside it must FLOW — a body carries through, the sleigh swings along its arc, a present falls a visible distance along a curve. If you can see the picture blink from one drawing to a different unrelated drawing, THE CLIP HAS FAILED.
AND IT IS BIG. At every moment at least one shape is TRAVELLING A LONG WAY across the lit rectangle — a third of its width or height — not wobbling on the spot.
FLASHING IS NOT MOVEMENT. Colour chases and strobes are allowed but they are secondary, and no more than one is running at a time. No all-over twinkle, no constant colour cycling, no strobing every element at once, no fizzing multicoloured noise. What the viewer sees is bodies and boxes moving, not lights blinking.
IT IS ALREADY RUNNING IN FRAME ONE: mid-gallop, mid-swing, presents already in mid-fall, at full speed. No still opening frame, no switch-on, no ease-in, no warm-up.

THE PRESENTS ARE THE HEADLINE — THEY ARE NEVER STILL, NOT ONE OF THEM, NOT FOR ONE FRAME. This is the single thing to get right after the continuous motion rule.
- IN THE AIR: presents burst up out of the sleigh and fall in LONG SMOOTH CURVED ARCS across the picture, each one tumbling end over end as it goes so you can see it rotating. AT MOST TWO ARE IN THE AIR AT ANY ONE INSTANT — a new one roughly every second, each one big enough to follow with your eye the whole way down. This is NOT a shower, NOT a rain of small objects, NOT confetti. Each falling present travels at least a third of the height of the lit rectangle, and it travels THROUGH every position on the way — it is never lifted from one spot and dropped into another.
- ON LANDING: a present does not simply stop. It HITS, SQUASHES FLAT for an instant, BOUNCES back up in a smaller arc, comes down again, and ROLLS or SLIDES sideways along the bottom before it comes to rest — the whole bounce-and-roll drawn as a continuous travelling movement.
- IN THE PILE: THE PILE AT THE BOTTOM IS ALIVE THE WHOLE FOUR SECONDS. It is NEVER a static heap. At every single moment at least two presents already in the pile are moving: one rocking back and forth, one sliding sideways, one tipping over onto its side, one being knocked along by the present that just landed on it, one squirting out sideways from under the pile like a pip. When the sleigh slams down or Santa crashes into the bottom of the picture, the whole pile JOLTS and several presents jump, scatter and resettle. The pile spreads SIDEWAYS along the bottom band as it grows, never upward, and it never rises past the lower third of the lit rectangle.
- THE TWO BURST-OPEN PRESENTS flap their lids up and down like little mouths as they tumble and as they sit in the pile.
If at any moment a viewer could freeze the clip and find the pile of presents looking exactly as it did a second earlier, THE CLIP HAS FAILED.

FOUR LAYERS RUN AT THE SAME TIME FOR THE WHOLE FOUR SECONDS, all of them big, continuous, large-amplitude movement:
LAYER ONE — THE TEAM AND THE SLEIGH. The four reindeer are not a frozen galloping decal: their legs scissor continuously, they rear, they crouch, they criss-cross, they squash short and stretch long, and the sleigh behind them rocks, slews, tips onto one runner and slams back down. The outline of the team and the sleigh is continuously changing shape, travelling through every in-between.
LAYER TWO — SANTA WHIPS AROUND ON THE END OF HIS ARM. Hanging off the sleigh by one hand, he swings out wide and back in, flips over onto his back, gets dragged boots-first, skis on one heel, is slingshotted up and slammed back down — and he SWEEPS through every position in between, never cutting. His two flying boots tumble end over end through the air the whole clip along smooth curved paths.
LAYER THREE — THE PRESENTS, exactly as described in the block above. This is the layer a viewer's eye lands on.
LAYER FOUR — THE ELF WORKS. The green elf in the back of the sleigh keeps hurling presents overboard — two-handed, one-handed, kicking one over the side — being flung up off his feet and landing again, his whole body carrying through each throw. THE STARS DO NOT CHASE, DO NOT RIPPLE AND DO NOT TWINKLE CONTINUOUSLY — they hold steady and only change on the few specific beats below that say so.

THE RUNAWAY, PHASE BY PHASE — ONE CONTINUOUS STORY:
0.0-0.5s — already mid-mutiny at full speed: the team stretched flat out and pouring forward, the sleigh slewing steadily over onto one runner, Santa swinging out wider and wider like a flag on a pole, one boot tumbling through the top of its arc, a present already falling and rotating on a long curve toward the bottom left. The elf winds up and hurls a second present overboard, following through with his whole body.
0.5-1.0s — the falling present lands, squashes, bounces and rolls sideways into the corner, knocking two presents already there skidding along ahead of it. The team lurches steadily left and the sleigh swings out wide behind it, dragging Santa right across the bottom of the picture from the left edge toward the middle, his beard streaming, his arm stretching comically long as he goes. Rudolph turns his head round to look back at him as it happens and his red nose swells brighter.
1.0-1.5s — Santa gets one boot down and skis along on his heel in one continuous slide, throwing up a spray of light as he travels, free arm windmilling. The sleigh slams back down flat and bounces; the whole pile of presents in the corner JOLTS and three of them jump, scatter and resettle. Another present bursts up out of the sleigh and starts its long arc down.
1.5-2.0s — the team splits into two pairs that criss-cross smoothly past each other and back into line, legs scissoring the whole way. Santa is flipped right over onto his back in one continuous roll and drags along boots-first, his hat sliding down over his face. The present in the air reaches the top of its arc and starts down. A single chase of colour runs slowly up the picture from the bottom.
2.0-2.5s — the reindeer rear up on their hind legs together in one smooth rise, the sleigh standing almost vertical behind them, the elf sliding down the tipping floor of it and grabbing on by his hat. The airborne present lands on the pile, bounces twice and slides; two more presents squirt sideways out from under it.
2.5-3.0s — they crash back down and pour forward again, and Santa is slingshotted up above the sleigh in one smooth rise with all four limbs spread, then swings down and across to the right-hand side of the picture. The elf sits down and tips a whole armful of presents over the side one after another, each one arcing down separately.
3.0-3.5s — those presents land in sequence, each squashing, bouncing and rolling, and the pile spreads visibly wider along the bottom. The team hauls steadily back up toward the top right and the sleigh whips round behind them, flinging Santa the full width of the picture back over to the left in one long unbroken swing. Rudolph lifts one front hoof mid-gallop and waves goodbye with it. The gold stars swap colour gold to white.
3.5-4.0s — the team squashes short and wide and stretches long and thin, Santa snapping back and forth on the end of his arm like a whip; his grip slips and he tumbles backwards in a ball, legs over his head, falling in one continuous arc into the pile of presents at the bottom, which explodes outward sideways — presents rolling, spinning, tipping and skidding in every direction along the bottom band. The elf waves both arms overhead in triumph. Presents are still rolling and rocking and the team is still galloping in the very last frame. Everything is still moving when the clip ends.

ABSOLUTE RULE — THE ARTWORK NEVER CHANGES SIZE AND NEVER BREAKS OUT OF THE CURTAIN. In the reference image the lit picture sits inside the curtain rectangle with a wide band of dark unlit garage door above it, below it and at both sides. THAT EXACT SIZE AND THAT EXACT POSITION ARE LOCKED from the first frame to the last. The team, the sleigh, Santa, the elf and the presents are the same number of bulbs tall and the same number of bulbs wide in the final frame as in the first frame. The artwork NEVER scales up, never grows, never inflates, never creeps outward and never drifts across the wall. There is ALWAYS a clear band of at least six unlit bulb rows of dark garage door between the highest lit point of the artwork — the tips of the reindeers' antlers — and the top edge of the curtain, and the same clear margin at the bottom and at both sides. No antler, no hoof, no runner, no boot, no present, no speed line and no star ever touches or crosses the edge of the curtain. Presents bounce and roll only INSIDE the rectangle and never fall out of the bottom of the curtain. Nothing lit ever appears outside the curtain rectangle — not on the terracotta stucco, the gable, the round attic vent, the roof, the sky, the hedge, the sconce or the driveway. If a pose would raise or widen the artwork past that margin, THE POSE IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER. The figures travel only inside the rectangle; they never exit one edge and re-enter at the other.

THE LIT RECTANGLE IS A FLAT PANEL OF LIGHT ON A FLAT DOOR. It is parallel to the garage door behind it and it moves with the door exactly as the door moves. It never detaches from the door, never floats forward, never becomes a stage, never becomes a doorway, never becomes a hole, and nothing ever comes out through it.

THE CROWD IS CASUAL, RELAXED AND ALREADY HAPPY — AND ALREADY MOVING IN THE VERY FIRST FRAME. This is not an audience waiting for something to happen. Nobody is standing frozen staring at the garage in anticipation, nobody holds still and then reacts, there is no build-up and no big synchronised cheer. They are neighbours hanging about on a driveway on a cold night, chatting to each other, laughing at each other, sipping drinks, glancing over at the lights and then back at their friends. Their attention drifts on and off the garage the whole time. They are ALL seen FROM BEHIND — no face is ever turned toward camera.
AT 0.0s, IN THE VERY FIRST FRAME, EVERY PERSON IS ALREADY MID-MOVEMENT. There is NO still opening frame, NO frozen first beat, NO pause before the motion starts.
CONTINUOUS OVERLAPPING MOTION, NEVER IN UNISON — at any instant several different people are doing several different things, and their little actions start and stop at different times so the group always looks alive:
0.0-0.5s — the person at the far left is already filming on a raised phone and shifts their weight to the other foot mid-shot; two adults beside them are mid-conversation, heads turned to each other, one shrugging.
0.5-1.0s — a woman in the centre in the long fur-collared coat laughs and rocks back on her heels, shoulders shaking; a child beside her bounces on the spot and swings an arm up toward the garage mid-bounce.
1.0-1.5s — the kids in the front row lean into each other and one points lazily at the lights without turning round; a teenager at the right shoves both hands deeper into their pockets, hunches, and blows out a long fog of breath.
1.5-2.0s — a woman at the left leans sideways into the person beside her and says something; they both laugh; another person scratches under their bobble hat and looks away from the garage entirely.
2.0-2.5s — two people knock shoulders and one claps once, casually; a child in a bobble hat twists round to tug a sleeve, then swings back; a couple sway slightly, arms linked.
2.5-3.0s — scattered easy laughter ripples through the group, a few loose claps at different moments, not together; one adult crouches down to say something to a kid; another points out at the lights with a cup still in hand.
3.0-3.5s — the person at the left lowers their phone, wipes their eyes laughing and says something sideways; a kid scrambles up onto their tiptoes; two heads tip together.
3.5-4.0s — a few people applaud loosely, hats bobbing, breath fogging, someone waves an arm overhead at a neighbour, someone else is already turning to talk to the person behind them.
Coats crease, hats bob, breath fogs, snow compresses under boots, shoulders shake with laughter. NOBODY IS EVER STILL and nobody is waiting.

COLOUR IS THE POINT: this stays a loud multicoloured light show the whole four seconds — red, green, blue, pink, orange, purple, yellow, turquoise and warm white all present and clearly separated at every moment. It must NEVER drop back to one or two colours.

Every change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off across the grid — chunky and stepped, like an animated light-bulb sign — but the movement it describes is continuous and flowing, never a jump between unrelated drawings. Coloured light spills and shifts across the driveway and over the backs of the crowd as the picture moves.

THE CURTAIN — CRITICAL: it stays a curtain of SEPARATE HANGING VERTICAL LIGHT STRANDS, NOT a solid LED video panel, NOT a flat screen and NOT a continuous mesh. There are clear DARK GAPS between the strands and the white garage door behind is plainly visible through those gaps, especially in the unlit band around the picture. The horizontal gap between neighbouring strands is visibly wider than the vertical gap between bulbs on the same strand, so the grid is tall and rectangular, never a square pixel matrix. The bulbs are small round points of light strung along thin BLACK cords. The unlit area is NOT a solid coloured field — it is simply dark garage door with dull grey unlit bulbs and faint dark cords, and there is NO filled background, NO coloured backdrop and NO saturated panel of colour behind the figures at any time. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT.

ABSOLUTELY NO TEXT: no letters, no words, no numbers, no speech bubbles, no signs and no writing of any kind anywhere in the lights or anywhere in the frame. No captions, no subtitles, no watermark.

AUDIO: natural outdoor sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Cold night air, a loose crowd chatting and laughing at different moments, scattered uneven clapping, boots in snow, breath fogging. No commentary, no voice-over.

LOOK: real iPhone night video, handheld, tack-sharp, candid UGC. Slight sensor noise in the shadows, no cinematic grade, no slow motion, no speed ramp. No text overlay, no watermark, no captions, no logo.

Reproduce the attached reference image EXACTLY - same vertical crop, same camera position and height, same composition, same lighting mood, same amateur phone-photo style. CHANGE ONLY ONE THING: the store is now heavily decorated for Christmas.

EVERYTHING BELOW STAYS EXACTLY AS IN THE REFERENCE:
- A vertical 9:16 photo taken on a phone from inside a crowd, held just above head height.
- FOREGROUND, bottom third of the frame: about twenty people seen strictly FROM BEHIND - backs of heads and shoulders, winter coats, hoodies, beanies and caps, packed together, slightly soft-focus, every one of them facing away from the camera and looking up. NOT ONE face is turned toward the camera.
- CENTRE of the frame: an enormous deep-red velvet curtain hung as a backdrop, very wide and very tall, filling the middle of the picture, heavy vertical folds, warm light hitting its middle so the centre glows a little brighter red than the edges. It hangs from a high horizontal rail.
- LEFT AND RIGHT EDGES: one man on each side standing high up on a tall aluminium A-frame step ladder, each holding a top corner of the curtain. They are small in the frame and seen at a distance. The man on the left wears a dark navy jacket and a cap; the man on the right wears an orange-red jacket and a cap.
- TOP of the frame: exposed industrial warehouse ceiling - corrugated metal roof deck, red-painted steel roof trusses and cross beams running overhead, hanging industrial pendant lights.
- BEHIND AND ABOVE the curtain: tall steel pallet racking stacked with cardboard boxes.

THE ONE CHANGE - dress the whole store for Christmas:
Thick green pine garland wound with red ribbon running along the red roof trusses and along the front rails of the pallet racking; warm white fairy lights strung across the ceiling beams; big red velvet bows on the racking uprights; oversized red and gold baubles hanging on ribbons from the ceiling beams; the boxes stacked on the shelves in red and green Christmas packaging; a fully decorated Christmas tree just visible at the far left behind the ladder. The decorations must sit naturally in the existing warehouse architecture - they do not change the camera, the crowd, the ladders, the men, or the size and position of the red curtain.

LOOK: a real photograph taken on a phone by someone in the crowd - natural mixed warehouse lighting, slightly blown highlights on the lit part of the curtain, mild grain, handheld imperfect framing.
DO NOT INCLUDE: any app interface, mute or speaker icon, text, captions, watermark, logo, or any face turned toward the camera. Not a render, not an illustration, no studio lighting.

THE BOXES ARE MATTE BLACK. They are NOT red. They are NOT green. They are NOT gold, white, patterned or generic Christmas packaging. Any red or green boxes already sitting on the shelves in the start frame are replaced by these matte black boxes in the revealed area. A wall of red boxes is a failed generation.

THE BOX, copied exactly from the reference image: a flat matte BLACK rectangular retail box. On the black front, top to bottom - a large white condensed uppercase headline LED CURTAIN LIGHTS; under it a warm gold subline Create Your Own Magic; a small red diagonal BEST SELLER ribbon flag in the top-right corner; one large photographic panel with a thin gold hairline border filling the middle and lower two thirds, showing a night photo of a suburban house facade lit with Christmas lights; and a bottom row of five small gold line icons. The box is closed and sealed - NO transparent window, NO clear plastic panel, NO cutout, nothing visible inside. The overall impression of the wall must be BLACK with white and gold print, not colourful.

One continuous take, 5 seconds, vertical 9:16, filmed on a phone held up over the heads of the crowd from exactly where the start frame is. NO zoom, NO pan, NO cuts. The crowd, the ladders and the warehouse stay in place the whole clip.

THE SCENE, unchanged from the start frame: a Christmas-decorated warehouse store. Bottom third, strictly FROM BEHIND, about twenty people in winter coats and beanies, facing away and looking up - not one face ever turns toward the camera. Centre, an enormous deep-red velvet curtain across the frame. Left edge, a man in a dark navy jacket and cap high on an aluminium A-frame ladder; right edge, a man in an orange-red jacket and cap on a matching ladder. Each grips a top corner of the curtain. Garland, fairy lights, baubles and red bows on the red roof trusses above.

ACTION - the timing must be exact and perfectly lip-synced:

0.0-0.8s - Both men shout "THREE" together. On that word they DIP the curtain DOWN and lift it straight back UP - one single down-up dip, hands dropping about forty centimetres and returning. The heavy velvet sags and bunches on the way down, stretches taut again on the way up.

0.8-1.6s - They shout "TWO" together. The same single down-up dip on the word, slightly deeper.

1.6-2.4s - They shout "ONE" together. The same single down-up dip on the word, the deepest of the three. The crowd below swells, phones going up.

2.4-3.0s - Both shout "Let's see how it comes out!" and on the last word they OPEN THEIR HANDS at the same instant and let go. The curtain collapses straight DOWN under its own weight, the top edge falling fastest, the velvet buckling and folding in on itself, the bottom pooling on the floor. Fast and heavy like wet canvas. It does NOT rise, drift sideways, fade or dissolve.

3.0-5.0s - THE REVEAL: tall steel pallet racking packed with the matte black product boxes described above, stacked face-out in tight neat rows and columns, filling the centre of the frame, their white headline and gold print catching the warehouse light against the black card. The crowd erupts - heads tipping back, arms and phones up - still every one seen only from behind. End frame: the packed racking of black boxes, fronts square to camera, print sharp and readable.

BOX SIZE: each box is a flat rectangle about 55 cm wide and 42 cm tall. FOUR stacked equal the height of a grown man. At least TEN fit side by side across the racking on every shelf and there are at least SIX shelf levels, so more than SIXTY boxes are visible at once - a dense grid of many small boxes, not a few large ones. Each is clearly much shorter than the men on the ladders. Every box is exactly the same size as every other, constant for the whole clip.

AUDIO: the two men's real voices shouting together, perfectly lip-synced and exactly on the beats above - "THREE" at 0.0, "TWO" at 0.8, "ONE" at 1.6, then "Let's see how it comes out!" - loud, excited, echoing in the metal warehouse. Under it: the fabric whump of the velvet on each dip, the ladders creaking, the crowd murmuring through the count then breaking into a real cheer and applause the instant the boxes appear. Warehouse reverb. NO music, NO whoosh or riser effects, NO narration.

REALISM: a real phone video shot in a crowd - handheld sway, a small jolt from being bumped, blown highlights on the pendant lights, crushed shadows in the roof trusses, sensor noise in the dark racking, real motion blur on the falling curtain and on raised arms, one quick autofocus hunt as the curtain drops.

BANNED: red boxes, green boxes, gold boxes, white boxes, generic or invented packaging, any box that is not the matte black box from the reference image; a transparent window or cutout in the box; warped or misspelled print; oversized or person-sized boxes; fewer than sixty boxes; empty shelves; the curtain rising on a count instead of dipping down first; the men letting go before the sentence ends; the curtain fading or dissolving instead of physically falling; any face in the crowd turning toward the camera; animation, cartoon, illustration or stylised imagery; cinematic colour grading; slow motion; any text overlay, caption, watermark or app interface.

ACTION TIMELINE - follow it exactly:

0.0-2.6s - THE COUNT. The two men on the ladders shout together, loud, over the crowd: "Three... two... one... let's see how it comes out!" On each of the three numbers they HEAVE the whole curtain upward and let it fall back - a big synchronised up-and-down bounce, arms straightening overhead on the lift and dropping on the release. The curtain is heavy velvet: it rises as one slow mass, the vertical folds stretching and opening, then sags and slaps back down, the bottom hem swinging in a wide lazy arc and rippling from the centre outward, each bounce a little bigger than the last. Their ladders flex and rock a few centimetres under the effort. The crowd in the bottom third swells - shoulders lifting, phones going up, a couple of arms raised.

2.6-3.1s - THE DROP. The instant the word "one" lands the men OPEN THEIR HANDS at the same moment and let go completely. The curtain is now unsupported: it collapses straight down under its own weight, the top edge falling fastest, the heavy fabric buckling and folding in on itself as it goes, air pushing out of it in a puff, the bottom pooling on the floor. It falls fast and heavy like wet canvas. It does NOT float, does NOT drift sideways, does NOT dissolve or fade out.

3.1-5.0s - THE REVEAL, AT TRUE SCALE. Behind where the curtain was, a huge floor-to-ceiling retail wall of the product boxes from the attached reference image is revealed - dozens and dozens of identical matte black boxes stacked face-out in tight rows and columns on the pallet racking and in pallet stacks on the floor, filling the whole centre of the frame. The boxes are closed and sealed - NO transparent window, NO clear plastic panel, NO cutout, nothing visible inside. SIZE IS CRITICAL: each box is about 55 cm wide, 42 cm tall and 10 cm deep - roughly as wide as a grown man's shoulders are across, so about three boxes stacked side by side span the width of one ladder's spread, and a person standing in front of the wall would be about four boxes tall. Every box is exactly the same size as every other one, constant for the whole clip. The two men stay on their ladders at the edges, looking at the wall of boxes. The crowd erupts - heads tip back, arms and phones shoot up - still every single one of them seen only from behind. End frame: the wall of boxes filling the centre, fronts square to camera, print sharp and readable.

AUDIO: the two men's real voices shouting the count together over the crowd, perfectly lip-synced, starting at the very first frame - loud, excited, echoing slightly in the metal warehouse. Under it: the heavy whump and fabric rush of the velvet curtain being heaved and then collapsing, the ladders creaking, and the crowd - a rising murmur during the count that breaks into a genuine cheer, whoops and applause the moment the boxes appear. Warehouse reverb on everything. NO music, NO whoosh or riser sound effects, NO narration.

REALISM - this must look like a real phone video shot in a crowd: handheld sway and a small jolt when the person filming is bumped; blown highlights on the warehouse pendant lights and crushed shadows up in the roof trusses; visible sensor noise in the dark racking; real motion blur on the falling curtain and on raised arms; one quick autofocus hunt as the curtain drops and the depth changes; slightly imperfect exposure that adjusts a little when the bright box wall is revealed.

1.2s — he throws both arms out level to the sides with his elbows bent, head tipped back, mouth a big open O, while the water in the tub chases colour left to right.
1.5s — he twists hard to one side, one shoulder driven forward, the cocktail held out level, and his sunglasses flash bright white twice.
1.8s — he twists hard the other way, the other shoulder forward, his beard swinging across, and a bright chase of colour runs down his whole body from hat to hands.
2.1s — he drops down low into the water until only his eyes, his hat and the raised cocktail are left showing, and every bubble twinkles out and back in a rolling wave from left to right.
2.4s — he pops straight back up with both arms braced on the rim of the tub and shimmies his shoulders left-right-left, the curls of steam flashing alternately.
2.7s — he kicks one boot up out of the water low and short to the side, arms bent and swinging opposite, his eyes wide open circles, and the wooden staves of the tub swap colour warm brown to red.
3.0s — he leans right back with both hands behind his head, elbows wide, one eye closed in a wink, and a crown of round droplets fires on all around the tub at once.
3.3s — he claps both hands together in front of his chest twice fast, his head bobbing with each clap, and the white fur trim on his suit and hat flashes brighter.
3.6s — he bounces in the tub twice, both arms bent up beside his head, the whole figure squashing and stretching with each bounce, and the stars and snowflakes flash on and off in alternating pairs.
3.9s — he lands square, cocktail raised, and the whole picture — figure, tub, water, bubbles, steam, stars and snowflakes — flashes to maximum brightness twice in quick succession for the final beat.
Every pose change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off row by row across the grid — chunky and instantaneous, never smooth morphing, never a crossfade. Coloured light spills and shifts across the snow below with every change.

ABSOLUTE RULE — THE ARTWORK NEVER CHANGES SIZE. When the door snaps on, the lit picture sits inside the curtain rectangle with a wide band of dark unlit cream door above it, below it and at both sides. THAT EXACT SIZE AND THAT EXACT POSITION ARE LOCKED from 0.7s to the last frame. Santa and the tub are the same number of bulbs tall and the same number of bulbs wide in the final frame as in the frame they first appear. He NEVER scales up, never grows, never inflates, never creeps upward, never fills more of the curtain than he does at the start. There is ALWAYS a clear band of at least six unlit bulb rows of dark door between the highest lit point of the artwork and the top edge of the curtain, and the same clear margin at the bottom and at both sides. No hand, no hat, no cocktail, no bubble, no droplet, no steam wisp and no star ever touches or crosses the edge of the curtain, and nothing lit ever appears on the navy siding, the white gutter, the roof, the sky, the firewood stack, the spruce or the driveway. If a pose would raise him or widen him past that margin, THE POSE IS DRAWN SMALLER AND LOWER — the artwork is never enlarged and the rectangle is never enlarged to fit it. BECAUSE HE CANNOT GROW, ALL HIS ENERGY GOES SIDEWAYS, DOWNWARDS AND INTO THE BULBS: twisting, sinking, leaning, bouncing, kicking low, flashing and chasing — never stretching upward. He also stays ON THE SPOT and centred; he never walks or slides sideways across the curtain.

CRITICAL — HOW THE LIGHTS MUST BE BUILT. The picture on the garage door is NOT an LED video wall, NOT a screen, NOT a projection. It is a hanging curtain of LED string lights: many thin vertical BLACK wires hung side by side from a header cable pinned under the garage lintel, each wire carrying small round bulbs. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT. The horizontal gap between neighbouring strands is visibly WIDER than the vertical gap between bulbs on the same strand, so the grid is tall and rectangular — never a square pixel matrix.
The curtain is SEE-THROUGH. Through the gaps between strands you clearly read the garage door behind it: its panel seams, its trim, the row of small square windows, the faint shadows of the wires falling on it. The unlit areas are NOT a solid coloured field — they are simply dark door, with the unlit bulbs showing as tiny dull grey dots and the thin BLACK wires as faint vertical lines.
Every lit bulb is a tiny point source: blown-out white core, coloured halo bleeding around it, a small soft star flare, individually countable. The artwork is extremely low resolution and blocky — chunky stepped edges, no smooth curves, no thin outlines, no small facial detail, no gradients, no anti-aliasing.
The curtain hangs a few inches proud of the door: the strands sag slightly, each strand ends at a slightly different height so the bottom hem is uneven and wavy, a faint breeze tilts the sheet, and the glow bounces back off the door behind it as a soft halo. Light spills onto the snow in a speckled uneven pattern, not a flat even wash.
ABSOLUTELY NO: LED video wall, TV screen, monitor, jumbotron, projector image, black backing panel, bezel or frame, dense square pixel grid, solid saturated background field, smooth clean vector artwork. Real amateur photo, natural noise, no CGI polish.

HOW THE PICTURE IN THE LIGHTS IS DRAWN: FLAT CARTOON LIGHT ART built out of individual glowing bulbs, like a chunky neon shop sign or a big pixel sprite. Thick simple outlines, flat blocks of colour, a big silly cartoon face. It is NOT a photograph, NOT a realistic person, NOT 3D, NOT rendered, NOT shaded, NOT textured, NOT lifelike, and NOT a video projected onto the door. Think cheap funny Christmas light display, not fine art.

HARD RULES: NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO SIGNS anywhere in the light artwork, and no captions, subtitles or watermark anywhere in the video. Nobody walks into frame.

AUDIO: natural outdoor sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Cold night air, a soft tap as the thumb hits the screen, the faint electrical hum and tick of a big light display, one quiet breath. No voice, nobody speaks.

LOOK: real iPhone night video, handheld, tack-sharp, candid UGC. Slight sensor noise in the shadows, no cinematic grade, no slow motion, no speed ramp. No text overlay, no watermark, no captions, no logo.

The last version cut between unrelated drawings — Santa in one pose, then suddenly a completely different unconnected pose, then another. That reads as a broken slideshow and it is exactly what must not happen.
The figures move the way a hand-drawn cartoon moves: EVERY FRAME IS AN IN-BETWEEN OF THE FRAME BEFORE IT. Arms, legs, heads and bodies TRAVEL THROUGH each movement along a smooth arc. A limb is NEVER teleported from one position to another. The picture NEVER cuts, NEVER snaps to an unrelated drawing, NEVER jumps, NEVER resets to the opening pose, NEVER freezes, NEVER pauses, NEVER settles and NEVER restarts as a loop.
It is ONE unbroken piece of comic business — a tug of war that escalates — running from the very first frame to the very last, and it is still running in the last frame. The story connects: each thing Santa does is caused by the thing that just happened to him.
THE TEST: play any half second of this clip and the motion inside it must FLOW — a body carries through, an arm sweeps, the reins snap and whip along their length. If you can see the picture blink from one drawing to a different unrelated drawing, THE CLIP HAS FAILED.

0.0-0.6s — already mid-haul: Santa leans back further and further, almost horizontal, both boots skidding steadily forward across the picture and throwing up growing sprays of snow, the reins stretching tighter and tighter. Rudolph doesn't budge — he only tips his chin higher and higher as Santa strains, his red nose swelling brighter.
0.6-1.2s — Rudolph suddenly shunts backwards, and Santa is hauled forward in one continuous lurch of about one body-width, up onto his toes, arms stretching out long in front of him, the slack reins whipping through a big travelling wave. He stays well inside the left half of the picture throughout — he does not cross the middle and he never nears the edge.
1.2-1.8s — Santa's boots lose their grip and he goes over backwards in one smooth topple — feet rising, body rotating, landing hard on his backside with both boots swinging up in the air — then rolls straight on over onto all fours without stopping, his hat sliding all the way down over his eyes on the way.
1.8-2.4s — still on his knees he crawls round behind Rudolph in one continuous move and starts shoving his backside with both hands, pushing with his whole body, his own boots slipping backwards under him a little further with every shove, while Rudolph slides sideways just a hand's width, plants himself and slowly blows an enormous raspberry, his cheeks ballooning out.
2.4-3.0s — Santa gives up, straightens up, pulls a big orange carrot out from behind his back and dangles it, swinging it slowly back and forth. Rudolph's head follows the carrot right to left, right to left, then he turns his nose away in one slow deliberate sweep and closes both eyes.
3.0-3.5s — Santa throws the carrot down, plants both fists on his hips, stamps one boot twice, grabs the reins again and hauls with one foot braced on Rudolph's backside, leaning right out sideways further and further.
3.5-4.0s — Rudolph stands straight up all at once, the reins go slack and Santa pitches forward onto his front and skids a short way along the bottom of the picture with his boots kicked up behind him — a short skid of about one body-length, ending well clear of the right-hand edge, never sliding off the picture. Rudolph sits calmly back down again. Santa picks himself up and drops down beside him in exactly the same sulking pose — arms folded, chin up — and Rudolph is still turning his head to wink one big cartoon eye as the clip ends. Everything is still moving in the very last frame.

Every change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off across the grid — chunky and stepped, like an animated light-bulb sign — but the movement it describes is continuous and flowing, never a jump between unrelated drawings. Coloured light spills and shifts across the snow and the cleared driveway as the picture moves.

ABSOLUTE RULE — THE ARTWORK NEVER CHANGES SIZE AND NEVER BREAKS OUT OF THE CURTAIN. In the reference image the lit picture sits inside the curtain rectangle with a wide band of dark unlit clapboard wall above it, below it and at both sides. THAT EXACT SIZE AND THAT EXACT POSITION ARE LOCKED from the first frame to the last. Santa, Rudolph and the reins are the same number of bulbs tall and the same number of bulbs wide in the final frame as in the first frame. No boot, no hoof, no antler, no rein, no carrot, no bobble, no snowflake and no star ever touches or crosses the edge of the curtain.
